# SwarmUI Maintainability Architecture Refresh

## Executive Summary

This refresh finds no justification for a wholesale rewrite. The current architecture has recognizable owners and the first four projects from the prior roadmap are complete: Image Editing has a dedicated UI coordinator, T2I route capabilities are split behind compatible surfaces, image-history behavior is decomposed behind one controller, and Swarm-maintained Comfy class/input contracts are cataloged. Those boundaries should remain closed unless new behavioral evidence appears.

The highest-value remaining work is reliability hardening at existing seams. The generic API/T2I diagnostic disclosure, transactional server-settings persistence, backend persistence protocol, custom-workflow durability/publication protocol, streaming producer-failure transport, model-load cleanup ownership, output transient-lifetime ownership, atomic process-shutdown ownership, backend shutdown task ownership, autoscaling launch-script platform validation, and lazy-tab descriptor consolidation are mitigated in production and maintainer-validated within their recorded environment boundaries; the browser and Comfy diagnostic mitigations and per-backend Comfy capability coordination are implemented awaiting their own maintainer validation. Other confirmed risks include generation/accounting cleanup gaps and several smaller lifecycle, cache, and output-state defects. These are independently bounded; combining them into a process-services, scheduler, frontend-module, or Comfy rewrite would increase regression risk without improving the first fixes.

Unranked security prerequisite `S1`, **redact generic server API/T2I submitted-input diagnostics**, is implemented and maintainer-validated. ## Scope, Exclusions, and Method

This refresh treats the current committed source at Task 1 base `b3af7a97052808c61dbabd73c322feec3c26d3b0` on `master` as authority. The governing audit design is `f34c31de0ae03c928320863386c2338558ced95b`, and the audit execution plan is `b3af7a97052808c61dbabd73c322feec3c26d3b0`. At the baseline, `master` was two commits ahead of `origin/master`; the index was empty; and the only tracked working-tree changes were `src/Data/Settings.fds`, `src/Pages/Text2Image.cshtml`, `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/gentab/loras.js`, and `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/main.js`, accompanied by the untracked `Data.pre-restore-2026-07-19/` directory. Those changes are not part of this audit.

The maintained-code scope is the core C# server, Razor and maintained browser code, built-in extensions, the Comfy adapter and Swarm-maintained Python under `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes`, repository launch/install/update tooling, and supporting maintained documentation and assets. The tracked-file inventory explicitly includes maintained hidden repository content under `.github/**`, root `.dockerignore`, `.gitattributes`, and `.gitignore`, `src/.editorconfig`, and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/.gitignore`. External extensions under `src/Extensions`, downloaded code under `dlbackend` and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/DLNodes`, generated API documentation, build and IDE output (`src/bin`, `src/obj`, and `.vs`), user state under root `Data`, `Output`, and `Models` and under `src/Data`, root `node_modules`, vendored static-library internals, byte-identical JavaScript `.bak` files, and backup content are excluded. Root `.codex` and `.superpowers/**` are excluded as agent/tool state. Root launchers and `launchtools` are read-only source evidence.

All inventory and source evidence in these sections is pinned to `b3af7a97052808c61dbabd73c322feec3c26d3b0`. The file inventory was derived from that tree with `git ls-tree`; counted maintained-source searches were rerun on only the included files materialized with `git show b3af7a97052808c61dbabd73c322feec3c26d3b0:<path>`, including the three protected dirty source paths; and architecture claims were revalidated with base-tree `git grep` and `git show`. The protected files' working-tree content was not read. All evidence is static: no build, test, launcher, installer, updater, server, browser automation, backend, or Comfy process was run. Counts state their boundaries and are navigation indicators, not findings. A statement directly established by declarations and call sites is treated as statically confirmed; behavior depending on runtime ordering, concurrency, browser timing, optional dependencies, or external extensions requires later measurement or maintainer validation. Later ranking follows, in order: compatibility and reliability; ownership and maintainability; measured or structurally demonstrated performance; and cosmetics.

## Current Architecture

### Repository and composition baseline

#### Runtime layers

SwarmUI has three principal maintained runtime layers: a classic-script/Razor browser frontend under `src/Pages`, `src/wwwroot/js`, and `src/wwwroot/css`; the C# application and Web/API host under `src`; and Python execution supplied chiefly by excluded upstream Comfy code, with Swarm-owned nodes confined to `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes` (`AGENTS.md`, `src/Core/Program.cs`, and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes`). Built-in extensions under `src/BuiltinExtensions` connect these layers without changing the executable composition root.

#### Process lifecycle

`Program.Main` is the process composition and lifecycle root (`src/Core/Program.cs`). After loading settings, it calls `Extensions.PrepExtensions`; that method discovers and instantiates extensions, then invokes `OnFirstInit` and `PopulateMetadata` through separate `RunOnAllExtensions` passes (`src/Core/ExtensionsManager.cs`). Back in `Program.Main`, the ordered phases continue with extension `OnPreInit`, model-list and default-parameter registration, construction of `BackendHandler`, `SessionHandler`, and `WebServer`, `Web.PreInit`, extension `OnInit`, model and backend loading, API registration through `BasicAPIFeatures.Register`, Web-host preparation, extension `OnPreLaunch`, and launch. `Program.Shutdown` invokes `PreShutdownEvent`, cancels `GlobalProgramCancel`, stops the Web host, backends, sessions, proxy, and model handlers, then calls extension `OnShutdown` and shuts down output metadata tracking. Process-wide owners remain publicly reachable through `Program.Backends`, `Sessions`, `T2IModelSets`, `Extensions`, `ServerSettings`, `Web`, and `GlobalProgramCancel`.

The exact-token maintained-C# consumer inventory, bounded to `src/**/*.cs` while excluding `src/Data`, external extensions, downloaded nodes, and build output, found: `Program.Backends` 64 occurrences in 18 files; `Program.Sessions` 58 in 8; `Program.T2IModelSets` 45 in 16; `Program.Extensions` 13 in 3; `Program.ServerSettings` 178 in 42; `Program.Web` 5 in 4; and `Program.GlobalProgramCancel` 91 in 25. These counts identify the breadth of the existing composition surface; they do not characterize those references as defective.

`PrepExtensions` first snapshots host dependency names, reads the maintained public-extension catalog, enumerates built-in and enabled external-extension directories, removes `.delete` directories with per-directory error reporting, discovers already loaded `Extension` subclasses, then builds/loads external projects under extension-specific load contexts. `PrepExtension` catches construction/path-discovery failures per type, and `RunOnAllExtensions` catches every phase exception per extension, so one extension failure is logged and later extensions continue. `OnFirstInit` and `PopulateMetadata` run only after discovery/build has completed. The later `OnPreInit`, `OnInit`, `OnPreLaunch`, and `OnShutdown` passes retain list order; that order, the hook names, and the timing of shared-registry contributions are extension compatibility contracts. Comfy uses `OnPreInit` for parameter/provider/model-refresh contributions, `OnInit` for additional parameters and conditional API registration, `OnPreLaunch` for launch-ready behavior, and `OnShutdown` to detach its dynamic parameter provider; Grid Generator registers parameters/permissions in `OnPreInit` and routes in `OnInit`.

Startup error handling is deliberately mixed by phase. Settings parsing catches readable command-line failures and returns before composition; optional update/git/hardware probes run through `RunCheckedTask` and are given a five-second aggregate startup wait; extension hooks are isolated per extension; model refresh catches per handler; and Web launch retries one known invalid host configuration before rethrowing other failures. `Extensions.PrepExtensions().Wait()`, the startup `Task.WaitAll`, `WebApp.WaitForShutdown`, and several process probes are synchronous composition boundaries rather than request dispatch. After launch, a cancellation-aware ten-second loop persists edited backend configuration, and the one-second utility tick thread invokes the registered tick/slow-tick owners under a loop-level exception boundary.

Shutdown can be entered from assembly unload, process exit, ASP.NET `ApplicationStopping`, `WaitForShutdown` completion, restart/control-timeout paths, CI boot, and permission-gated `AdminAPI.ShutdownServer`, which schedules a delayed direct call. It now begins with the rank-13 atomic one-way ownership gate recorded in Core F10; only the zero-to-one winner continues. The winner sets a nonzero requested process exit code after a bounded webhook wait, invokes `PreShutdownEvent`, cancels `GlobalProgramCancel`, stops ASP.NET, backends, sessions, proxy, and each model handler, then invokes extension shutdown, closes output-metadata databases, removes the process temp directory, and flushes logs. Extension shutdown remains per-extension isolated, while the composition-level calls before and after it are sequential. Cancellation is signalled before Web/backend/session disposal so background loops and backend work can observe the token; session removal and interruption use their own per-session cancellation sources. Maintainer validation confirmed the recorded overlap behavior on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, on 2026-07-27.

#### Settings and process persistence

`Settings` is an `AutoConfiguration` tree whose public fields define persisted names and reflection metadata. `Program.LoadSettingsFile` reads FDS, applies legacy key migrations for logging, metadata, autocomplete, role limits, and maintenance, then loads the singleton `Program.ServerSettings`; a missing or malformed file is logged and leaves defaults/current values. At the pinned audit base, early startup re-saved normalized settings unless locked, then applied command-line overrides and runtime log settings. The then-void `SaveSettingsFile` respected `LockSettings`, wrote the entire configuration, and mirrored `Maintenance.AutoPullDevUpdates` into the launcher-consumed `src/bin/always_pull` marker; save failures were logged and not thrown. Core F6 records the later observable implementation.

At the pinned audit base, runtime server edits entered through `AdminAPI.ChangeServerSettings`: reflective field lookup rejected unknown and hidden fields, secret placeholders were ignored, authorization enablement had a lockout guard, accepted values mutated `Program.ServerSettings`, and the route saved before path validation/rebuild and `ReapplySettings`. Installation mutated theme/network/install/language fields, saved once installation completed, reloaded backends, and restarted. Extension enable/disable routes mutated `DisabledExtensions` and saved before a restart applied discovery changes; `ExtensionsManager` also removed no-longer-installed names when managing this list. Backend settings have their own `Backends.fds` lifecycle: `BackendHandler.Load`, the post-launch dirty loop, explicit backend edit routes, shutdown, and `Installation` reload are the maintained persistence/process consumers. Core F6 records both the confirmed base defect and its later corrected implementation; Core F7 separately records model-catalog replacement during a path edit.

#### Web/API composition

`WebServer` owns ASP.NET/Razor composition and request routing (`src/Core/WebServer.cs`). `WebServer.Prep` configures middleware, response compression, static files, WebSockets, Razor Pages, shutdown integration, the `/API/{*Call}` dispatcher, output/view routes, extension files, and audio. `API.APIHandlers` is the route registry; `API.RegisterAPICall` adds either an `APICall` or a reflected delegate; and `API.HandleAsyncRequest` owns HTTP/WebSocket parsing, session resolution, permission enforcement, reflective dispatch, and response/error transport (`src/WebAPI/API.cs` and `src/WebAPI/APICallReflectBuilder.cs`). `BasicAPIFeatures.Register` registers account/session/general routes and composes `T2IAPI`, `ModelsAPI`, `BackendAPI`, `AdminAPI`, `UtilAPI`, and `PromptLabAPI` (`src/WebAPI/BasicAPIFeatures.cs`). Static registration searches found 147 `API.RegisterAPICall` sites across 11 maintained C# files, including built-in Comfy, Grid Generator, and Image Batch Tool contributions.

Before endpoint mapping, `WebServer.Prep` installs origin-observation/dev-forwarding middleware, optional CORS preflight handling, optional exact `Authorization`-header enforcement with configured bypass IPs, compression, exception handling, static files, routing, and WebSockets. It maps Razor Pages without antiforgery, `/API/{*Call}`, `/Output`/`/View`, `/ViewSpecial`, `/ExtensionFile`, and `/Audio`, then installs a terminal 404 redirect. Output/audio/special routes resolve a user separately through `WebServer.GetUserFor`/`WebUtil.GetValidLogin`, validate roots with `CheckFilePath`, enforce other-user/model restrictions, and use structured JSON refusal responses. They do not pass through `API.HandleAsyncRequest`.

The API registry key is the lowercase reflected method name, and `Dictionary.Add` makes duplicate route names a fail-fast registration error. Built-in extension `OnInit` registration occurs before `BasicAPIFeatures.Register`: Grid Generator contributes six routes and Image Batch Tool one; Comfy conditionally calls `ComfyUIWebAPI.Register` from its `OnInit` and contributes twelve. Core general/account routes register first inside `BasicAPIFeatures`, followed in fixed order by the T2I, model, backend, admin, utility, and Prompt Lab groups. `T2IAPI.Register` remains the ordered facade for generation, history, Krita, classic-inpaint, parameter-list, and refresh routes. External extensions may register in the same `OnInit` phase, so method names, signatures, registration ordering relative to core, permission objects, and duplicate-name failure behavior are compatibility constraints.

`APICallReflectBuilder` accepts only `Task<JObject>` methods. `HttpContext`, `Session`, `WebSocket`, and a root `JObject` are injected; the root object is copied with `session_id` removed. Primitive and `string[]` arguments bind by exact C# parameter name, use the reflected default when absent, and otherwise return an `error` object on failed coercion. `IDataHolder` parameters accept either a nested object under the parameter name or the root object, apply field names/required metadata, and support only registered coercer types. A `WebSocket` parameter marks the whole route as WebSocket-only. Consequently route method names, parameter names/types/defaults, `IDataHolder` field names, API attributes, and whether a socket parameter exists are wire/reflection contracts.

`API.HandleAsyncRequest` accepts one bounded WebSocket JSON message or a non-empty bounded JSON POST; other HTTP methods redirect to `NoGetAPI`. Except for the explicit lowercase sessionless set (`login`, `getnewsession`, `registerbasic`, `registeroauth`, and `getactivekritasession`), it resolves `session_id` from JSON or `X-Session-ID`, rehydrates persisted sessions through `SessionHandler.TryGetSession`, ticks user presence/update time, then checks the registered permission before binding. Unknown routes, transport mismatch, missing/invalid sessions, and permission failures have structured IDs, but HTTP and WebSocket status/close paths differ. Handler-returned `error` objects are logged and still transported as normal handler output (HTTP 200); thrown exceptions are logged with detail and converted to generic `internal_error`. `RunWebsocketHandlerCallWS` now drains queued stream frames and then transports an unexpected producer fault as one generic `internal_error` frame plus a `false` result for its route owner; Core F5/rank 10 is implemented and maintainer-validated on Linux. Frontend F4 records the pinned-base client-side full-input disclosure; its mitigation is implemented and awaiting maintainer validation. Server dispatch does not log the complete request object on ordinary handler errors, though parser exceptions, reflective validation/error messages, and route-specific logs can include submitted values as separately bounded below.

#### Current extracted boundaries

The current T2I route owner is already split by capability: `T2IAPI.Register` preserves the route-composition surface while registering methods owned by `ImageHistoryAPI`, `KritaAPI`, and `ClassicInpaintAPI` alongside generation and parameter/model-refresh methods (`src/WebAPI/T2IAPI.cs`, `src/WebAPI/ImageHistoryAPI.cs`, `src/WebAPI/KritaAPI.cs`, and `src/WebAPI/ClassicInpaintAPI.cs`). On the browser side, `ImageHistoryController` remains in `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/gentab/outputhistory.js` and composes `ImageHistoryFilter`, `ImageHistoryComparison`, and `ImageHistoryBulkActions` from `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/gentab/imagehistoryfilter.js`, `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/gentab/imagehistorycomparison.js`, and `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/gentab/imagehistorybulkactions.js`; the Image Editing coordinator is owned by `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/helpers/image_editor_ui.js`. These are current committed boundaries, not assumptions carried forward from the 2026-07-20 audit.

#### Extension composition

`ExtensionsManager` discovers built-in extension types from loaded assemblies and external extension projects from `src/Extensions` and instantiates `Extension` subclasses (`src/Core/ExtensionsManager.cs`). Its `RunOnAllExtensions` performs the per-phase iteration requested by callers; `Program` establishes the lifecycle phase order. The `Extension` base contributes `ScriptFiles`, `StyleSheetFiles`, `OtherAssets`, metadata, and `OnFirstInit`/`OnPreInit`/`OnInit`/`OnPreLaunch`/`OnShutdown` hooks (`src/Core/Extension.cs`). Extensions also contribute through shared registries such as `API.RegisterAPICall`, `T2IParamTypes.Register`, `BackendHandler.RegisterBackendType`, permissions, and `WorkflowGenerator` step lists. `WebServer.GatherExtensionPageAdditions` converts extension script/style/asset lists and `Tabs/Text2Image/*.html` files into served resources and generated page fragments. Thus `Program` owns lifecycle order, `ExtensionsManager` owns discovery, instances, and per-phase iteration, each `Extension` owns its contribution declarations, and `WebServer` materializes browser-facing contributions.

#### Backend/session/model state

Backend ownership is centered on `BackendHandler`: `AllBackends` is a concurrent configured-instance registry, `BackendTypes` is the type catalog, `Load` and `Shutdown` own configured lifecycle, and `GetNextT2IBackend` plus request/pressure registries coordinate T2I acquisition and model-loading pressure (`src/Backends/BackendHandler.cs`). `SessionHandler` owns concurrent session, user, and role registries plus persistent login sessions (`src/Accounts/SessionHandler.cs`). Each `T2IModelHandler` owns a concurrent model catalog and refresh/shutdown behavior, while `Program.T2IModelSets` maps model categories to those handlers (`src/Text2Image/T2IModelHandler.cs` and `src/Core/Program.cs`).

`SessionHandler` opens `Users.ldb`, prepares weekly bounded backups, exposes LiteDB collections for users, API sessions, login tokens, presets, generic data, and OAuth lookup, and loads `Roles.fds` under a single `DBLock`. Default roles are synthesized, new permission IDs registered by core/extensions are propagated according to their declared defaults, and the owner wildcard remains authoritative. `API.HandleAsyncRequest` checks the session's stacked `Role` immediately before invocation; parameter-level permissions are checked later by `T2IParamTypes.ApplyParameter`. API sessions are secure-random IDs held in concurrent process/user maps and optionally persisted; rehydration verifies that an originating login token still exists. `Session.GenClaim` tracks queue/live counters with interlocked updates and combines the session interrupt with a local claim interrupt. `Session.Interrupt` cancels the current token source and installs a new one for later work. Persistent browser login tokens are hashed in LiteDB and validated from the HttpOnly cookie by `WebUtil`; revocation removes the login record and associated API sessions. Core F8 records the historical unbounded, non-consuming pending OAuth challenge behavior; Rank 19 now provides an expiring, bounded, atomically consumed lifecycle and is implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Firefox (version not provided). Core F9 records the implemented and maintainer-validated alignment of the historical OAuth cookie flag difference.

`Program.BuildModelLists` derives seven core handler categories from configured roots/subfolders and exposes them through the public `Program.T2IModelSets` dictionary. Each handler subscribes to `Program.ModelRefreshEvent`; `Refresh` validates roots, recursively scans directories in parallel, builds a new concurrent model map, loads cached or embedded/sidecar metadata, then replaces that handler's map under `ModificationLock`. Model names are filtered by the session's role at list/option time. Metadata is cached in LiteDB either centrally or per folder; record reuse is now gated by the model file's last-write timestamp and an optional persisted fingerprint of the four ordered metadata sidecars, while recomputation inspects safe-tensor/GGUF headers, preview candidates, and those sidecars. Legacy null fingerprints recompute once. Some `ModelsAPI` operations use `Program.RefreshLock` read claims and its download path takes a later write claim; `T2IAPI.TriggerRefresh` uses a write claim, a semaphore, and a ten-second redundancy window. `AdminAPI.ChangeServerSettings` rebuilds the outer model-set dictionary without that lock, as detailed in Core F7. Core F12's sidecar-freshness correction is implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Firefox (version not provided); duplicate sidecar I/O/parsing when recomputation occurs remains the separate measurement-gated and unimplemented Core P4.

#### Parameter registry and request conversion

`T2IParamTypes.Types` is the public process-wide parameter registry keyed by cleaned ID. `Register<T>` fixes the ID, wire data type, and `SharpType`, fails on duplicate identity through `Dictionary.Add`, appends language strings, and returns the public typed `T2IRegisteredParam<T>` handle stored in core/extension fields. `RegisterDefaults` owns core groups and default parameters; built-ins add their own parameters during `OnPreInit`/`OnInit`. `ParameterRemaps` preserves four retired IDs, `FakeTypeProviders` allows context-sensitive extension parameters (Comfy supplies the maintained provider), and public fields/groups/list identities are source and mutable-object compatibility surfaces rather than candidates for bulk encapsulation.

`T2IParamType.ToNet` emits the browser contract, including ID/name/type/subtype/default/range/options/display/group/retention fields, and evaluates session-specific option providers. `ListT2IParams` permission-filters types, collects reachable groups, serializes model classes/model lists, and returns parameter edits and remaps. `FromNet` supports remote reconstruction but does not round-trip every server-only delegate/permission field. Option validation uses cleaned exact/best matches, numeric bounds, booleans, lists, and model names restricted through `ListModelNamesFor(session)`. File-backed media references are root-checked, special-history paths are resolved, then the pending-save cache or filesystem is converted to a data URL; Core P3 records that synchronous critical-path mechanism.

`T2IAPI.RequestToParams` creates a session-bound `T2IParamInput`, orders `AlwaysTopKeys` first for extension routing needs, copies `extra_metadata`, ignores transport/preset keys, warns and skips unknown keys, converts arrays to the list separator, and calls `T2IParamTypes.ApplyParameter`/`ValidateParam` for validation, permission checks, parsing, model resolution, and required-feature accumulation. Selected presets are then applied or retained for Grid Generator. Its five maintained caller contexts are core `T2IAPI` generation, `ImageHistoryAPI.AddImageToHistory`, `ComfyUIWebAPI.ComfyGetGeneratedWorkflow`, Grid Generator `GridGenRun`, and Image Batch `GenBatchRun_Internal`. `T2IParamInput` owns the typed set, section overrides, extra metadata, feature/refusal state, pending presets, session interrupt token, unique request ID/time, and queried-parameter tracking. Early handlers normalize resolution/LoRA lists and early preset model selection; late handling parses prompt-like syntax. On the normal engine path, that late pass runs on its task input before backend acquisition; conditional `<preset>`/`<param>` tags can re-enter parameter validation and file-backed media conversion. Comfy workflow preview and `TestPromptFill` also invoke the late pass once. Clones duplicate mutable sets/lists but retain session/cancellation and referenced model/media values. Metadata and reusable API JSON are derived from the same typed set while respecting hide/save/unused contracts.

#### Core utilities and focused helpers

`Utilities` is a public compatibility toolbox, but its maintained consumers fall into coherent domains: startup/tick/restart and GC coordination (`Program`, `MemCleaner`, status/webhooks); WebSocket/HTTP JSON framing and safe debug formatting (`API`, WebServer, backends, built-in APIs); filesystem/path/content-type/recycle helpers (models, image history, sessions, installation); downloads, process/git execution, port/platform checks, and .NET/ffmpeg discovery (installation, Admin/Backend/Models APIs, backend launchers); and random/hash/password helpers (sessions/users and metadata). This breadth is navigation evidence, not a reason to relocate the class wholesale.

Focused owners already exist where behavior is cohesive. `WebUtil` owns Razor/HTML helpers, platform warnings, forwarded-IP cleaning, and persistent-login cookie validation; `PythonLaunchHelper` owns Python environment/path/process setup; `MetadataHelper` owns image metadata encoding; `OutputMetadataTracker` owns per-folder LiteDB metadata/history indexes plus a bounded 512-entry/120-second preview memory cache; `KritaImageBridge`, `WildcardExpansionHelper`, `WildcardsHelper`, `AutoCompleteListHelper`, `UserSoundHelper`, `ProxyHandler`, and `WebhookManager` own their respective stateful operations. Errors range from explicit refusal tuples/JSON and contextual logging to best-effort catches for cache cleanup/platform discovery; process and network helpers generally propagate after adding context. Core F11's bounded `DownloadFile` lifecycle correction is implemented and maintainer-validated on Linux across all 14 approved cases. No standalone utility reorganization is supported by consumer evidence.

#### Comfy adapter

The Comfy built-in is the adapter boundary between C# generation and Python nodes. `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIBackendExtension.cs` registers assets, parameters, backend types, lifecycle work, and feature discovery; `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyCapabilityCatalog.cs` supplies selected detected-node-to-feature mappings. `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyNodeNames.cs` catalogs Swarm-maintained node class names, while `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyNodeInputNames.cs` catalogs approved schema-backed input keys used by built-in code. These are bounded compatibility catalogs, not inventories of every Comfy class name, input, or string. Their maintained consumers include `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/WorkflowGenerator.cs`, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/WorkflowGeneratorSteps.cs`, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/WorkflowGeneratorModelSupport.cs`, and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/WGNodeData.cs`, which build Comfy JSON graphs and ordered generation steps. On the Python side, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes/SwarmComfyCommon/__init__.py` composes common `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes/SwarmComfyExtra/__init__.py` conditionally composes optional mappings, and node modules such as `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes/SwarmComfyCommon/SwarmSaveImageWS.py`, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes/SwarmComfyCommon/SwarmKSampler.py`, and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ExtraNodes/SwarmComfyExtra/SwarmSaveAnimationWS.py` define registration entries and `INPUT_TYPES` schemas. Downloaded Comfy and node repositories remain outside maintained scope.

#### Launch/install/update surfaces

The read-only launcher surface has distinct platform paths. `launch-linux.sh` and `launch-macos.sh` source `launchtools/linux-build-logic.sh`, which repairs the .NET path, optionally pulls, rebuilds when commit markers demand it, retains a failed-build backup, and sets host environment before the launcher starts the built server; exit code 42 re-enters the launcher. `launch-windows.bat` carries equivalent pull/build/backup/start/restart logic, while `launch-linux-dev.sh` and `launch-windows-dev.ps1` force debug rebuild paths. `launchtools/install-linux.sh` and `install-windows.bat` install prerequisites, clone SwarmUI, and hand off to the platform launcher. Docker launch scripts build the selected Dockerfile, mount the applicable repository/data/backend paths, and call inner scripts that return to `launch-linux.sh`. Separately, runtime installation and updates are owned by `Installation.Install` and Admin API routes such as `CheckForUpdates`, `UpdateAndRestart`, extension update/install methods, and `InstallDotnetUpdate` (`src/Core/Installation.cs` and `src/WebAPI/AdminAPI.cs`).

### Frontend, Razor, and Web composition

#### Layout and classic-script order

`src/Pages/Shared/_Layout.cshtml` is the browser composition root. In the head it emits, in order, the selected dark/light Bootstrap stylesheet, Select2 styles, `site.css`, the page `Header` section, extension styles through `WebServer.PageHeaderExtra`, and the selected theme styles. It then loads jQuery, Bootstrap, Select2, `util.js`, and `translator.js`. After the page body it loads `permissions.js`, then `site.js`, then the page `Scripts` section. Consequently generation-page code can rely on utility, translation, permission, request, and session globals, while theme CSS has later cascade position than core, page, and extension CSS. This ordering is a compatibility contract; the maintained CSS searches found responsive rules, fixed-position overlays, z-index layers, and `!important` overrides, but those constructs are contextual evidence of the existing cross-theme/browser contract rather than findings.

`src/Pages/Text2Image.cshtml` supplies the generation-page order inside that `Scripts` section. After the excluded EXIF dependency, the base sequence is `welcomemessages.js`, `browsers.js`, presets, wildcards, base `loras.js`, models, params, prompt tools, utility/user-tab definitions, `generatehandler.js`, Simple tab, layout, metadata, generation controls, current-image handling, the three image-history collaborators, `outputhistory.js`, notes, base `main.js`, Prompt Lab, settings editor, UI improvements, and media controls. `WebServer.PageFooterExtra` then appends extension scripts; `finalscript.js` is last and schedules `genpageLoad` with a one-millisecond timeout. Static source establishes declaration and scheduling order, not when a particular browser will execute the queued timer relative to unrelated work.

`Extension.ScriptFiles`, `StyleSheetFiles`, and `OtherAssets` are public contribution lists populated by extensions (`src/Core/Extension.cs`). `WebServer.GatherExtensionPageAdditions` iterates `Program.Extensions.RunOnAllExtensions`: scripts and styles become cache-varied `ExtensionFile/{ExtensionName}/...` tags, other assets become served binary entries, and `Tabs/Text2Image/*.html` files become permission-gated top-tab header/body fragments. It assigns the resulting strings to `PageFooterExtra`, `PageHeaderExtra`, `T2ITabHeader`, and `T2ITabBody` (`src/Core/WebServer.cs`). Text2Image places extension tab headers between Image Editing and Utilities, extension bodies after the Image Editing shell, and extension scripts immediately before `finalscript.js`. Extension order, classic-script globals, tab IDs, and the final-script position must therefore remain compatible even though external extensions are outside the auditable consumer inventory.

#### Generation-page initialization, session callbacks, and lazy tabs

`genpageLoad` in base `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/main.js` first binds Bootstrap top-tab handlers, initializes `genTabLayout`, starts a status request, and starts hash restoration. It then calls `getSession` from `site.js`. A successful `GetNewSession` sets `session_id`, `user_id`, output-prefix behavior, permissions, and server-version state before invoking the callback. The generation callback builds the history shell, requests `ListT2IParams`, loads model classes/models/wildcards and parameter definitions, applies parameter configuration, creates inputs/tools, restores UI state, and runs `sessionReadyCallbacks` in insertion order. Core scripts register the server-lazy-tab callback in `main.js`, Prompt Lab initialization in `promptlab.js`, and settings-editor initialization in `settings_editor.js`; PageFooter extension scripts then register maintained Comfy workflow preparation, Grid Generator registration, and Image Batch Tool registration in extension iteration order. Excluded external extension scripts may also use the global array. The successful tail starts the Krita poll, welcome/title work, marks `swarmHasLoaded`, and schedules initial history loading. A `ListT2IParams` failure warns and still schedules history, but does not run the successful tail. There is no maintained `postSessionReadyCallbacks` definition or consumer at the pinned base.

`genericRequest` and `makeWSRequest` both attach the current session and reacquire one after an `invalid_session_id`; these request-level retries do not rerun the one-time `sessionReadyCallbacks`. `getSession` throttles checks within one second and reports a server-version change. Separately, status polling suppresses hidden-page requests until at least thirty seconds have elapsed since the last hidden-page poll. While the server version remains unchanged, a response with a non-empty status class selects a two-second interval and an empty class selects sixty seconds, independently of document visibility; the `versionIsWrong` branch updates the displayed error but preserves the previously selected interval. Static source supports this reconnect/request behavior; it does not establish runtime network timing.

Text2Image's `lazyTabs` list emits placeholder panes and `window.genpageLazyTabs`; `window.genpageLazyScriptGroups` gives Image Editing the ordered `color_picker.js`, `image_editor_tools.js`, `image_editor.js`, and `image_editor_ui.js` group and gives Server its ordered backend/server/log scripts. In `main.js`, `loadScript` memoizes by exact `src`, creates non-async script elements, and rejects with the source path; `ensureLazyScriptGroup` awaits each source in manifest order. `ensureLazyTabMarkup` then calls permission-gated `GetGenPageTabPartial`, replaces the shell with returned HTML, enables sliders, and applies translations. `activateLazyTab` performs scripts, markup, and the tab hook exactly once per successful activation; failures clear activation/loading state for retry. Bootstrap `show.bs.tab` assigns a monotonically increasing request ID, `shown.bs.tab` activates the lazy tab, and stale request IDs prevent later sub-tab/hash work from an older open request. `UtilAPI.LazyGenPageTabPartials` is the server allowlist and permission check for the same four keys (`src/WebAPI/UtilAPI.cs`).

#### Browser ownership and global compatibility surface

The requested top-level declaration audit shows distinct but classic-script-visible owners. `site.js` owns session/user/output state, request transports, common input widgets, media-file input, prompt indicators, and error display. `util.js` owns predominantly pure DOM/string/cookie/media helpers and publishes `window.SwarmUtil` as a compatibility namespace over selected existing functions. `currentimagehandler.js` owns image-card registration, full/current/batch image state, current-image actions and comparisons. `params.js` owns generated parameter DOM/configuration and `getGenInput`; `models.js` owns model records, browsers, metadata editing, and `currentModelHelper`; `generatehandler.js` defines `GenerateHandler`; base `main.js` composes `mainGenHandler`, startup/session state, status/features, editor readiness, lazy tabs, and top-tab/hash behavior; base `loras.js` owns `SelectedLora` and `LoraHelper`. `outputhistory.js` owns `ImageHistoryController`, its browser/request/selection state, and history compatibility delegates.

The exact maintained search-consumer boundaries below cover `src/**/*.js` and Razor at the pinned base while excluding external extensions, downloaded nodes, generated/build/user state, backups, and vendored libraries. Definitions are separated from actual consumers:

| Boundary | Definition/owner | All maintained search consumers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `genericRequest` | `site.js` | Built-in Comfy workflow and Grid Generator assets; `ServerTab.cshtml`; current-image, generation-controls, history bulk/comparison/controller, models, notes, params, presets, wildcards, generate-handler, image-editor tools, settings editor, `main.js`, Prompt Lab, backend/log/server tabs, Simple/User/Utilities tabs, `translator.js`, and internal `site.js` session/prompt/input-image paths. |
| `makeWSRequest` | `site.js` | `generatecontrols.js` through `makeWSRequestT2I`, `models.js`, `utiltab.js`, and `installer.js`; the recursive call is internal to `site.js`. |
| `getGenInput` | `params.js` | Built-in Comfy workflow, Grid Generator, and Image Batch Tool assets; current-image, generation-controls, output-history, `GenerateHandler`, image-editor tools, base `main.js`, and Simple tab. `GenerateHandler.getGenInput` is a facade; `SimpleTabGenerateHandler` supplies its separate override. |
| `setCurrentImage` | `currentimagehandler.js` | its own card/batch/drop flows, `GenerateTab.cshtml`, output-history selection/comparison callbacks, `GenerateHandler`, base `main.js` Krita import, and Simple tab through its handler. |
| `window.SwarmUtil` | `util.js`, forwarding to existing utility implementations | base `main.js`, Prompt Lab, Simple tab, Utilities tab, and `translator.js`. The original function globals remain loaded first for compatibility. |
| `ImageHistoryController` | class and sole construction in `outputhistory.js` | no maintained direct consumer outside `outputhistory.js`; collaborators receive callbacks, while global delegates/accessors expose compatibility operations. |
| `GenerateHandler` | `generatehandler.js` | instantiated as `mainGenHandler` in base `main.js` and subclassed by `SimpleTabGenerateHandler` in `simpletab.js`. |
| History compatibility exports | `registerMediaButton`, `notifyImageHistorySavedPath`, `imageHistoryBrowser`, and `registeredMediaButtons` in `outputhistory.js` | `notifyImageHistorySavedPath` is consumed by built-in Grid Generator, current-image save/result paths, and `GenerateHandler`; `imageHistoryBrowser` and `registeredMediaButtons` are consumed by `currentimagehandler.js`; `registerMediaButton` is documented as extension-facing, with no other maintained in-scope caller. |

The request/session globals and generation-page arrays are intentional classic-script composition surfaces where Razor and extensions participate. In contrast, the controller/collaborator instances and their mutable sets/tokens are implementation state and are not directly consumed outside `outputhistory.js`. The `imageHistoryBrowser` accessor deliberately exposes the exact controller-owned browser, and `registeredMediaButtons` deliberately exposes its array; those are compatibility exports, not duplicate owners.

#### Image Editing and output-history ownership

`src/wwwroot/js/genpage/helpers/image_editor_ui.js` now owns the Image Editing tab's zoom, persisted panel/splitter state, color/tool/control wiring, tab editor, Generate-editor transfer, and activation/deactivation coordination. Text2Image lazy-loads its dependencies in class-definition order. `main.js::ensureGenerateImageEditorReady` uses the same group to construct the separate Generate-tab `window.imageEditor` on first use. `currentimagehandler.js::runImageEditingCoordinatorAction` ensures the group before calling the coordinator's `openGenerateTabEditorForImage` or `sendToImageEditingTabPreview`. The Generate-tab editor also has two direct maintained consumers: `layout.js` sizes the editor/current-image split, calls `resize`, and reads editor geometry while dragging its splitter; `params.js` synchronizes width/height changes, refreshes parameter visibility, and substitutes the active editor's final image, mask, and rounded dimensions into generation input. The lazy Image Editing partial's feature handlers resolve to coordinator functions; its generic slider handlers continue to use shared `updateRangeStyle`. The coordinator deactivates the Generate editor when Image Editing activates, deactivates its tab editor when another top tab is shown, and restores the Generate editor when appropriate. Its namespaced `shown.bs.tab.sendLayersToGenerateEditor` handler removes any earlier copy before registering and removes itself after the Generate tab appears.

`ImageHistoryController` in `outputhistory.js` owns the `GenPageBrowserClass`, foreground/background tokens, retry/watchdog/saved-refresh timers, selection, busy state, media-button registry, and history window manager. It composes `ImageHistoryFilter` (bounded 1,024-entry parsed-metadata cache and compiled query), `ImageHistoryComparison` (modal/pan/reveal/diff/metadata state), and `ImageHistoryBulkActions` (selection snapshots and asynchronous mutation/export/contact-sheet/Prompt Lab workflows). Collaborators receive callbacks rather than controller fields; bulk asynchronous work uses `try`/`finally` through `runBusy`. The controller's stale request tokens, request key, bounded retries, watchdogs, and timer clearing are explicit current reliability controls. `outputhistory.js` retains global inline-handler delegates and browser/media-button accessors for Razor/current-image/extension compatibility.

### Backend, generation, and output composition

#### Backend catalogs, persistence, and lifecycle

`BackendHandler` owns the process-wide backend-type registry, the `AllBackends` concurrent instance catalog, real/non-real ID allocation, `Data/Backends.fds` serialization, queued and fast initialization, availability signals, request/model-pressure state, and loaded-model annotations (`src/Backends/BackendHandler.cs`). Core types are registered in its constructor; built-in extensions register additional types during `OnPreInit`. Real instances are loaded from FDS at startup and mutated through `BackendAPI`; `Installation.BackendComfy` also directly adds the real `comfyui_selfstart` backend. Non-real instances are runtime-only children used by owners such as `AutoScalingBackend` and controlling `SwarmSwarmBackend` instances; their stop/refresh/shutdown paths also call the shared `DeleteById`, although `Save` excludes them. The public `Program.Backends` facade and `BackendHandler.BackendData`/`T2IBackendData` records are consumed by generation, status/admin APIs, model refresh, utilities, and built-ins, so type IDs, backend IDs, settings field names, status values, real/non-real visibility, and the facade remain compatibility contracts.

`Load` starts the serialized initialization monitor and the request-handling thread. Fast-capable backends initialize through tasks; other instances enter `BackendsToInit`, whose monitor retries according to `MaxBackendInitAttempts`, reports long-running load stages, and refreshes model-loaded flags. `ShutdownBackendCleanly`, reload, edit, delete, and process shutdown coordinate through `ShutDownReserve`, usage/model-load reservations, per-backend `DoShutdownNow`, and the handler signals. The ten-second Program loop persists the public dirty flag, while shutdown conditionally performs a final save. Backend F14 and F21 retain the distinct dirty-signal and save-failure gaps; the historical F15 load-completion gap is implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs. Core F10 remains the outer process-shutdown gate.

#### Scheduling, claims, model pressure, and autoscaling

`T2IEngine.BackendMatcherFor` derives an acceptance predicate from requested backend type/ID, feature flags, model inventories, LoRAs, embeddings, backend-specific validation, and extension validators. `GetNextT2IBackend` registers a `T2IBackendRequest` and waits on its event under the request/session cancellation token and timeout. The single request loop snapshots pending requests, rejects cancellation, invokes extension scheduling gates, and calls `TryFind`: available instances are filtered by status/reservations/usage, an already loaded requested model is preferred, and a successful choice returns a disposable `T2IBackendAccess` whose constructor increments usage and whose disposal decrements it and signals the scheduler.

When no available backend has the requested model, requests contribute to a per-model `ModelRequestPressure`. `LoadHighestPressureNow` orders pressure by wait/count heuristic, selects a compatible available loader while tracking failed backend IDs/reasons, reserves the chosen backend, creates model-load claims for participating sessions, waits for active usages, loads the model, then releases claims/reservation and refreshes model-loaded flags. No-backend/no-match paths make at most one autoscaling attempt before producing a readable failure; a queue-wide timeout can restart all backends or fail requests according to settings. `AutoScalingBackend` registers a `NewBackendNeededEvent`, enforces configured count/time/queue gates under `ScaleBehaviorLock`, launches runtime child backends through the configured script, pings and stops idle children from the one-second tick, and removes its hooks/children on shutdown. Backend F13 records the historical ineffective platform-extension guard and its implemented correction, maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Bash (version not provided), with Windows runtime unvalidated, while F17 records cancellation before model-load work starts.

#### Generation orchestration and output persistence

`T2IAPI.GenT2I_Internal` owns the request claim, raw-to-typed parameter conversion, per-image clones/seeds, concurrency task list, streamed progress/results, mini-grid construction, post-batch event, discard list, and terminal webhook. Each `T2IEngine.CreateImageTask` applies late prompt logic and pre-generation hooks, acquires one backend access, and invokes `GenerateLive` inside that disposable usage scope. A media callback synchronously invokes post-generation hooks, metadata preparation, and the API save/output callback before control returns to `GenerateLive`; only after generation and those callback paths complete does the `using` release backend usage. Background conversion/persistence started by the callback can outlive that claim. Readable refusal/timeout paths become user errors; unexpected generation exceptions are logged and converted to the generic generation error. A temporary backend redirect re-enters acquisition with tool preprocessing disabled while preserving claim accounting; the maintained Comfy thrower marks itself `IDLE` first so the next scheduler pass excludes that instance.

`Session.ApplyMetadata` constructs the serialized parameter/extra metadata, records final dimensions, and optionally converts image format on a task. `T2IAPI` bypasses `Session.SaveImage` and creates a data URL for `DoNotSave` outputs and intermediates covered by `DoNotSaveIntermediates`. When it does call `Session.SaveImage`, user-level `SaveFiles = false` returns a data URL before path resolution, locking, or scanning. Only the persisted branch resolves the user's output template and history mapping, chooses a collision-free extension-bearing path while holding `UserLock`, publishes a pending byte task in `StillSavingFiles`, and starts background persistence. That task writes media and optional `.swarm.json`, creates/loads a preview, upserts `OutputMetadataTracker`'s LiteDB history index, retains pending bytes for ten seconds for immediate `/Output`/`/View` reads, then removes the pending entry. `OutputMetadataTracker` owns per-root/per-folder LiteDB metadata, preview and history-index collections under database locks, plus a bounded in-memory preview LRU. A persisted output's URL is streamed before background disk/index completion; current-image/history notification then reaches the browser through the existing frontend flow. Backend F19 and F20 record the two distinct output transient-state lifecycles.

### Comfy integration, workflow generation, and managed Python

#### Extension lifecycle, storage, discovery, and transport

`ComfyUIBackendExtension` remains the Comfy composition root. In `OnPreInit` it establishes `Folder`, loads the workflow-file manifest, subscribes model refresh/path-change handlers, contributes browser assets and dynamic parameter providers, removes obsolete Python caches, reserves raw-workflow top keys, seeds presumptive optional features from downloaded-node directories, populates model dropdowns, and subscribes Swarm-remote discovery. `OnInit` registers the API/self-start backend types, Comfy routes/update callbacks, and Comfy parameters; `OnPreLaunch` maps `/ComfyBackendDirect`; `OnShutdown` removes the dynamic provider. `ComfyUIWebAPI` permission-gates feature-install and Torch-update routes that delegate to `ComfyUISelfStartBackend` process/package helpers; the extension also contributes Comfy/core/node Git checks and updates to the Admin API's update workflow. This audit inspected those paths but did not execute them.

Stored workflows now use `ComfyWorkflowStore` as the production transaction and maintained-access owner behind the extension/API facades. Save, overwrite, rename, and delete commit through same-filesystem staging, a single durable journal, and recoverable originals; cache publication follows durable commit, and later cleanup is content/hash-verified. Recovery and refresh use one fail-closed gate shared by maintained readers. Staged refresh repopulates the existing public `CustomWorkflows` dictionary instance under the same lock and gate, preserving its identity and lazy-record compatibility, while persistence/recovery diagnostics remain content-redacted. Comfy F23 is implemented and maintainer-validated. Comfy P8 remains a separate measurement prerequisite for cold-list hydration and authorizes no cache or performance change.

Each `ComfyUIAPIAbstractBackend.LoadValueSet` now serializes its `object_info` refresh, builds complete raw-info, node, model, folder, shared-value, and capability candidates, and publishes one immutable backend-local capability snapshot through the extension's locked `AssignValuesFromRaw`. Generation, model loading, preview, direct workflow routing, and validation consume the selected backend's snapshot. Linked remotes retain remote-status scheduling authority and publish a separate frozen node set for raw validation. The registry reconciles public compatibility mutations and republishes the same-object global feature aggregate for parameter visibility; maintained readers do not use that mutable set for backend-local decisions. This is Comfy F22/rank 8's implemented boundary pending maintainer validation. `ComfyUIRedirectHelper` proxies HTTP/WebSockets for the embedded Comfy UI and merges backend `object_info` behind a ten-minute cache; Comfy F24 records the implemented and maintainer-validated warm-fallback correction, with environment and backend topology not provided.

Normal generation calls `CreateWorkflow`; a stored/raw workflow receives tag substitution, while the standard path constructs a `WorkflowGenerator` and executes its ordered steps. `AwaitJobLive` reuses or opens a Comfy WebSocket, submits `/prompt`, translates execution/progress/preview/final-output messages, deletes or interrupts canceled jobs through `/queue`, `/interrupt`, and `/history`, and returns healthy sockets to a pool. `GenerateLive` also uploads image inputs when Swarm base64 nodes are unavailable and runs the registered removal-or-ID-release actions in `finally`. Raw/direct workflows are checked against each backend's `NodeTypes`; the standard generated path relies on feature selection and Comfy validation. At the pinned audit base, diagnostic paths exposed filled tag values, complete generated/direct-proxy workflow or prompt JSON, truncated typed-input values, ControlNet base64 prefixes, and parser fragments through exactly nine statements. Comfy F25 now records those nine original sinks separately from 12 submitted/private parsing or unescaping boundaries and the additional raw browser WebSocket statement; the production mitigation is implemented and awaiting maintainer validation.

#### Workflow construction and extension surface

The stateful `WorkflowGenerator` spans `WorkflowGenerator.cs` (3,405 lines), `WorkflowGeneratorModelSupport.cs` (1,493), and `WorkflowGeneratorSteps.cs` (2,451), with `WGNodeData.cs` adding 562 lines of typed media/model path conversion and output assembly at the pinned base. These measurements are navigation context, not findings. A generator instance owns the mutable `Workflow` graph, typed current model/text/VAE/media trackers, compatibility properties, mode flags, model list, helper-node cache, sequential `LastID` starting at the documented reserved boundary of 100, stable dynamic-ID searches, and graph connectivity cache.

The public static `Steps` and `ModelGenSteps` lists contain priority-ordered `Action<WorkflowGenerator>` delegates. The type initializer registers core anonymous steps; built-in Dynamic Thresholding adds a step in its `OnInit`; and external extensions may use `AddStep`, `AddModelGenStep`, public methods, legacy properties, and direct generator state. Main steps execute in ascending priority until `SkipFurtherSteps`; model steps are split at priority `-100`. Fixed IDs below 100, `GetStableDynamicID` regions, equal-priority registration order, JSON property values/order, node output indexes, and graph rewrites are compatibility contracts. Comfy F26 therefore limits the first ownership extraction to graph creation/traversal/rewrite primitives behind the existing generator facade, rather than reopening the entire generator.

#### Managed Python and C#/Python contracts

Swarm-maintained Python is confined to `ExtraNodes`. `SwarmComfyCommon/__init__.py` composes the regular feature modules' `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`; ClipSeg is imported under an exception boundary. `SwarmComfyExtra/__init__.py` independently catches `ImportError` for RemBG, animation/FFmpeg, YOLO, and TunableOp modules, so their mapping keys exist only when dependencies import successfully. The Python mappings and `INPUT_TYPES`/modern schemas remain runtime authority; `object_info` is the C# observation of actual availability. Exceptions raised by node implementations flow through Comfy's prompt/execution error protocol, while import failures are reported in Comfy's process logs.

The completed C# boundary is deliberately static and partial: `ComfyNodeNames` contains 65 Swarm-maintained registered class-name constants; `ComfyNodeInputNames` contains 184 schema-backed keys under 46 node-scoped classes used by maintained C#; and `ComfyCapabilityCatalog` owns construction/application of the existing mixed Swarm/upstream node-to-feature map. Maintained consumers are confined to the catalog/capability owner, backend abstract adapter, extension, Web API, `WGNodeData`, and the three workflow-generator files. Catalog membership does not claim runtime availability, does not cover upstream node strings, and does not replace Python/object-info authority.

### Inventory indicators

The reproducible inventory boundary starts with every tracked path returned by `git ls-tree -r --name-only b3af7a97052808c61dbabd73c322feec3c26d3b0`. It explicitly includes `.github/**`, root `.dockerignore`, `.gitattributes`, and `.gitignore`, `src/.editorconfig`, and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/.gitignore`. It excludes root `Data`, `Output`, `Models`, `node_modules`, `Data.pre-restore-2026-07-19`, and `.codex`; `.superpowers/**`; `src/Data`; external extensions under `src/Extensions`; downloaded code under `dlbackend` and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/DLNodes`; generated API documentation under `docs/APIRoutes`; generated or IDE output under `src/bin`, `src/obj`, and `.vs`; the eight byte-identical backups `src/wwwroot/js/translator.js.bak`, `src/wwwroot/js/registerpage.js.bak`, `src/wwwroot/js/loginpage.js.bak`, `src/wwwroot/js/installer.js.bak`, `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/main.js.bak`, `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/promptlab.js.bak`, `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/simpletab.js.bak`, and `src/wwwroot/js/genpage/utiltab.js.bak`; and eight vendored static files: `src/wwwroot/js/lib/bootstrap.min.js`, `src/wwwroot/js/lib/exif-reader.min.js`, `src/wwwroot/js/lib/jquery.min.js`, `src/wwwroot/js/lib/select2.min.js`, `src/wwwroot/css/bootstrap.min.css`, `src/wwwroot/css/bootstrap_light.min.css`, `src/wwwroot/css/select2.min.css`, and `src/wwwroot/css/select2_bootstrap.min.css`. This boundary contains 528 files, distributed as follows:
