## Primary Cross-Layer Flows

### Browser boot, session establishment, and lazy tabs

1. `_Layout.cshtml` resolves the theme and emits base/page/extension/theme CSS, then jQuery/Bootstrap/Select2, `util.js`, and `translator.js`. After the Razor body it emits `permissions.js`, `site.js`, and Text2Image's ordered script section.
2. `Text2Image.cshtml` emits server-derived feature toggles, Comfy installable-feature data, parameter remaps, lazy-tab descriptors/script groups, eager Generate/Simple/optional Prompt Lab markup, lazy shells, and extension tabs. Its scripts define shared browser helpers and feature owners; extension scripts follow the maintained scripts; `finalscript.js` queues `genpageLoad` last.
3. `genpageLoad` binds Bootstrap/hash/lazy-tab behavior, initializes layout/status, starts `loadHashHelper`, and calls `site.js::getSession`. `GetNewSession` updates session/user/output/permission/version state; invalid-session replies to later HTTP and WebSocket requests reacquire a session and retry the request, without rerunning generation-page startup callbacks.
4. On session success, `ListT2IParams` supplies models, wildcards, parameters/groups, and parameter edits. `main.js` composes those through `modelsHelpers`, `buildParameterList`, `paramConfig`, `genInputs`, and `genToolsList`, then invokes `sessionReadyCallbacks` in insertion order and schedules the first history load.
5. A user click, hash restoration, or `openGenPageTabAsync` assigns a top-tab request ID. For a lazy tab, `activateLazyTab` awaits its script group in declared order, calls `GetGenPageTabPartial`, injects the allowlisted/permission-checked Razor partial, enables sliders/translations, and invokes the tab-specific hook. Stale request IDs suppress obsolete continuation and hash work; rejected script/markup work is reset for a later retry.
6. Image Editing's hook initializes its controls/editor; Utilities and User call their lazy initialization/refresh functions; Server initializes server/log helpers and conditionally loads backend types. Server resource and log intervals are gated by tab visibility/document visibility. Extension tabs are eager HTML fragments and extension scripts are classic scripts, not participants in the core lazy-tab manifest.

### Generated output, current image, and history

1. `GenerateHandler.doGenerate` collects parameters through its `getGenInput` facade (base owner `params.js`), preloads the selected model when needed, and opens or reuses `GenerateText2ImageWS` through `makeWSRequestT2I`/`makeWSRequest` (`generatehandler.js`, `generatecontrols.js`, and `site.js`). `internalHandleData` owns request/batch tracking, progress/preview replacement, completion, interrupt cleanup, and socket-intention close handling.
2. Preview messages call the handler/current-image preview path and create or update generating cards without notifying history. A final image either calls `gotImageResult` for a new card or `gotTrackedImageResult` for a tracked card. Those paths update the visible current/batch image through `currentimagehandler.js` and call global `notifyImageHistorySavedPath` when present.
3. `notifyImageHistorySavedPath` delegates to `ImageHistoryController.notifySavedPath`. The controller rejects data/placeholder paths, normalizes the output path, records it in a selected target set, attempts an optimistic insertion against the active folder/depth/filter/sort state, and schedules bounded confirmation refreshes. Current-image explicit save and built-in Grid Generator completion use the same compatibility notification.
4. Independently, `main.js` schedules the history browser's initial navigation after parameter startup, and the History bottom-tab event also calls `scheduleInitialLoad`. `ImageHistoryController.listFolderAndFiles` makes a fast-first root `ListImages` request capped at 128, maps/sorts/filters and renders it, then makes a background request without an explicit client limit. `ImageHistoryAPI` caps both indexed and scan-path returned files at the user's `MaxImagesInHistory` setting (default 1,000), so the background result is the configured returned cap or fewer matching entries rather than the total history. Foreground load tokens and background token/request-key checks discard stale responses; one initial retry, two background retries, and a ten-second watchdog provide bounded recovery/status behavior.
5. A committed full response replaces `GenPageBrowserClass` contents and its `lastFiles`/`lastFilesMap` state. `ImageHistoryFilter` parses metadata through its bounded cache and supplies synchronous matcher/search fields; `ImageHistoryWindowManager` coalesces scroll/resize work and hydrates media near the visible rows. Selecting a history entry delegates back to controller `selectOutput`, which calls `setCurrentImage`; comparison and bulk handlers snapshot controller selection and delegate to their collaborators.
6. Single-image and bulk mutations use `genericRequest`, update controller/browser/card state through callbacks, and request coalesced refreshes. Bulk workflows serialize requests deliberately per selected image except the explicit `BulkMoveImages` route, retain partial-success counts, and release busy state in `finally`. The `imageHistoryBrowser`, `registeredMediaButtons`, and handler globals preserve current-image and extension-facing classic-script compatibility.

### Process startup, extension phases, and shutdown

1. `Program.Main` initializes localization/LiteDB/network/logging, attaches unload/exit/unhandled-exception handlers, parses environment/arguments, resolves `DataDir`/settings, applies legacy migrations, normalizes and saves settings, then applies command-line/runtime overrides. Invalid readable command-line input logs usage and returns; settings read/save failures retain defaults/current state and continue.
2. `Extensions.PrepExtensions().Wait()` snapshots host dependencies, loads the public catalog, discovers core/enabled external directories and types, builds/loads external projects, instantiates extensions, and then runs `OnFirstInit` and `PopulateMetadata`. Discovery/build/type errors are isolated and logged at extension granularity; hook errors are isolated by `RunOnAllExtensions` while preserving list order.
3. `OnPreInit` runs before model handlers/default parameter registration, allowing permissions, parameters, backend types, dynamic providers, and lifecycle events to exist before core catalogs are finalized. Program then creates `BackendHandler`, `SessionHandler`, and `WebServer`; `Web.PreInit` establishes core themes; `OnInit` contributes extension APIs/assets/state before default permissions are applied and before `BasicAPIFeatures.Register` adds core routes.
4. Program initializes utilities/languages/models/wildcards/autocomplete/sounds, loads configured backends, registers core APIs, builds the Web application and extension page additions, runs `OnPreLaunch`, fixes permission display order, and starts Kestrel. Optional update/git/hardware tasks are allowed a bounded five-second aggregate wait; failures are logged without changing the completed composition phases.
5. After launch, the backend dirty loop saves every ten seconds under global cancellation, the utility tick thread invokes one-second/minute events, and `WaitForShutdown` holds the main thread. Restart, ASP.NET stopping, assembly unload, process exit, CI, control timeout, or permission-gated `AdminAPI.ShutdownServer` (after its half-second scheduled delay) can enter `Program.Shutdown`.
6. Shutdown first atomically selects one zero-to-one owner; later callers return immediately. The winner waits at most two minutes for the shutdown webhook, assigns only a requested nonzero exit code, invokes pre-shutdown network work, cancels the process token, and then stops Web, backends, sessions, proxy, model handlers, extensions, metadata databases, temp files, and logs in that order. Extension exceptions are per-extension isolated. Core F10 records the implemented and maintainer-validated gate; backend/session/model-specific cancellation and disposal behavior otherwise remains with those owners.

### API dispatch, permission, binding, and error transport

1. ASP.NET middleware first applies configured origin/CORS/auth behavior, static/dev forwarding, routing, and WebSockets. `/API/{*Call}` enters `API.HandleAsyncRequest`; output, special-view, extension-file, audio, and Razor requests use their separate route owners and authorization/path checks.
2. The dispatcher accepts one WebSocket JSON message or a JSON POST within `MaxReceiveBytes`. It lowercases the path after `/api/`; sessionless names bypass session lookup, while every other name must supply `session_id` or `X-Session-ID`. `SessionHandler.TryGetSession` checks memory first, then rehydrates a persisted session under `DBLock` only if its originating login token remains valid.
3. The lowercase path selects the registered `APICall`. Transport mismatch is rejected before invocation. For a session route, the dispatcher updates presence, updates last-used time when `IsUserUpdate` is true, and checks the exact registered `PermInfo`; parameter-specific permissions remain a later `T2IParamTypes.ApplyParameter` concern.
4. The reflection-built mapper injects context/session/socket/root JSON and binds remaining inputs by exact parameter or data-holder field name. Missing required/invalid primitive inputs become handler-style `{ error }` objects; reflected defaults are wire defaults. Registered method name, `WebSocket` presence, parameter names/types/defaults, attributes, user-update flag, permission, and registration order must remain stable for generated docs, clients, extensions, and direct reflection consumers.
5. HTTP handler output must be non-null and is serialized with status 200 even when it contains `error`; transport, session, route, and permission refusals use structured 400/401/404 responses depending on branch, while reflection-binding failures are handler output and retain status 200. WebSocket handler output is optionally sent, then the socket is closed normally. Unexpected exceptions are logged with server detail and transported as generic `internal_error`; premature remote socket closure is logged without a second response.
6. Streaming owners enqueue frames through `RunWebsocketHandlerCallWS`, which sends them in order until the producer completes. The implemented Core F5/rank 10 path preserves queued output, then logs an unexpected producer fault in detail, emits one generic `internal_error` frame, and returns `false` so `GenerateText2ImageWS`, `SelectModelWS`, Comfy LoRA/TensorRT routes, and Image Batch Tool suppress contradictory finalization. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the complete Linux fault, coordination, preserved-behavior/security, and successful-flow matrix on 2026-07-25. Frontend invalid-session retry behavior remains Frontend F1; Frontend F4 retains the implemented browser payload-redaction boundary and pending validation record.

### Generation request parameters, models, and session claims

1. `GenerateText2Image` or `GenerateText2ImageWS` arrives with an API-resolved `Session`, an `images` binding, and the remaining root `JObject`. `GenT2I_Internal` creates a disposable `Session.GenClaim` before calling `RequestToParams`; the claim links queue/live counters to the session interrupt and owns a local cancellation source used for request refusal.
2. `RequestToParams` creates `T2IParamInput`, processes extension-defined `AlwaysTopKeys` first, imports extra metadata, then resolves each cleaned key through fake providers, registered types, or `ParameterRemaps`. `T2IParamTypes.ApplyParameter` checks per-parameter permission, calls `ValidateParam` to resolve/coerce allowed models/media paths and other values, writes the typed set/section, and accumulates required backend feature flags. Unknown keys are logged and skipped; invalid readable values become generation `error` output. This outer raw/request-preset conversion completes before the per-image `T2IEngine.CreateImageTask` work and backend scheduling.
3. Presets apply after raw keys unless the Grid Generator asks to retain them. Early special handlers normalize resolution, LoRA weights/text-encoder weights/schedules, and model-bearing prompt presets; the input then fixes image count/claim size, seed behavior, prompt parsing, metadata, and queried/unused tracking as generation owners consume it.
4. Model parameters consult the category in `Program.T2IModelSets`, obtain the session-filtered name list, choose the clean best match, and resolve a `T2IModel`; optional configured metadata hashing can synchronously ensure the tensor hash. Media path references are constrained to the user's output root and special-history resolution before data conversion. The sync read/wait/base64 mechanism is Core P3.
5. The orchestration clones the typed input per image/batch offset, increments seeds, and schedules `T2IEngine.CreateImageTask` work up to the user's calculated backend limit. On its normal pre-backend path, an engine task calls `T2IParamInput.ApplyLateSpecialLogic`; only prompts containing applicable tags re-enter preset/parameter application, and file conversion occurs only when those tags apply file-backed media. Backend acquisition and refusal reasons remain downstream owners. Output callbacks apply metadata, save or emit data URLs, update history metadata/indexes, run webhooks, stream image/progress/discard frames, and delete a refused saved file.
6. Session interruption cancels the current `SessInterrupt` source and installs a new source for later requests; disposing each claim removes it and reverses its counters. WebSocket disconnect/cancel handling and the process token feed their respective linked/local cancellation paths. HTTP generation buffers streaming-style outputs through `RunWebsocketHandlerCallDirect` and returns surviving image URLs; WebSocket generation preserves incremental frames and its socket-intention reuse window.

### Backend acquisition, model loading, generation, and history handoff

1. Each per-image engine task applies conditional late prompt logic and extension pre-generation hooks, then extends its session claim as a backend wait and submits a `T2IBackendRequest` with the requested model, full matcher, session, load-notification callback, timeout, and session-interrupt token.
2. The request-handling thread wakes on `CheckBackendsSignal` or its one-second fallback, snapshots pending requests, applies extension scheduling gates, and filters current backends by enabled/running/reservation/capacity state. A match with the requested model is claimed immediately through `T2IBackendAccess`; otherwise the request joins model pressure or asks registered autoscalers for capacity. Failure and cancellation signal the same completion event consumed by `GetNextT2IBackend`.
3. The pressure owner selects the highest compatible waiting model, reserves a loader, accounts model-load claims to participating sessions, waits for existing usages, and calls the backend's `LoadModel`. Success makes the next request-loop pass claim the backend; failures accumulate backend IDs/reasons until a readable all-loaders-failed refusal is possible. Backend access remains claimed throughout the `GenerateLive` call and all synchronous media-callback work.
4. `GenerateLive` callbacks translate media into `ImageOutput` or forward progress JSON. For media, the callback synchronously invokes post-generation hooks; an accepted output gets cloned request metadata and an optional conversion task. `T2IAPI` creates a data URL directly for `DoNotSave` and covered `DoNotSaveIntermediates` results; otherwise it calls `Session.SaveImage`, which also returns a data URL before path work when user `SaveFiles` is false and enters path selection/persistence only when saving is enabled. The API streams the resulting URL/metadata and updates batch/discard bookkeeping. The callback returns to `GenerateLive`, and backend usage is released only when `GenerateLive` and the surrounding `using` complete. Conversion and any background persistence may continue after release; the API later constructs an optional mini-grid and invokes post-batch hooks.
5. For outputs that enter persisted saving, the background task writes final bytes and an optional metadata sidecar, populates preview and history-index state, and retains pending bytes briefly so `/Output`/`/View` and file-backed parameter resolution can serve a just-announced path before disk reads are necessary. The browser's current-image handler then calls the history compatibility notification, while subsequent `ListImages` uses the persisted index or scan/rebuild path. Output filenames, data-URL and persisted-URL behavior, URL prefixes, metadata shape/sidecars, index relative paths, preview behavior, result-frame order, and refusal/discard semantics are compatibility boundaries.
6. Session interruption, per-request refusal, backend timeout, backend redirection, backend deletion/reload, autoscaler stop, and process cancellation touch different layers of this flow. The retained backend findings below keep concurrency-limit enforcement, pressure cleanup, shutdown tracking, pending-save cleanup, and filename reservation bounded to their current owners rather than proposing one combined scheduler/lifecycle rewrite.

### Comfy discovery, workflow construction, execution, and optional nodes

1. A Comfy backend initializes by fetching `object_info`. `LoadValueSet` derives that instance's node-name set, model lists, and path-separator convention, then passes complete candidates to `AssignValuesFromRaw` for shared dropdowns, preprocessors, feature mapping, hook-scheduling detection, extension parsers, and immutable owner publication. F22 retains the historical global-state mismatch and records the implemented per-backend snapshot plus separately coordinated compatibility aggregate now awaiting maintainer validation.
2. A normal request selected for that backend enters `ComfyUIAPIAbstractBackend.GenerateLive`. `CreateWorkflow` first rewrites prompt embedding syntax. A selected stored/raw workflow is tag-filled from typed input—including dynamic parameters, models, media, seeds, LoRAs, and the user's Stability API key tag—while a standard request instantiates `WorkflowGenerator` with the selected backend's advertised features. The generated-workflow preview route logs `T2IParamInput.ToString`, which retains the first 256 rendered characters of each value before adding an ellipsis. Separately, the standard ControlNet step serializes every `ValuesInput` key through `ToJSON`, including media converted to base64, then `ToDenseDebugString` retains 253 characters plus an ellipsis for each over-limit scalar before the missing-ControlNet/init-image error. Malformed direct workflows and direct prompts also pass through `Utilities.ParseToJson`, which replaces the original parser failure with a `JsonReaderException` containing the cleaned first 256 submitted characters; outer `ReadableString()` diagnostics expose that wrapper text. F25 includes both distinct truncated typed-input sinks and both parser-exception sinks.
3. Standard generation executes ascending registered steps over one mutable graph. Model steps construct/load the current model, text encoders, and VAEs; later steps build conditioning, inputs, masks/control/adapters, samplers/refiners/video, and output nodes. `CreateNode` preserves fixed/reserved IDs or advances `LastID`, and `WGNodeData` converts typed model/media paths and emits the appropriate image, animation, or audio save node. A final cleanup step scans/reconnects/removes redundant nodes. External workflow steps receive the same generator facade and ordering/ID conventions.
4. Swarm-owned `class_type` and consumed input names come from the completed C# catalogs; Python `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` and schemas define their execution. Regular common modules load as one package except separately guarded ClipSeg; dependency-heavy Extra modules register only after successful imports. `object_info` remains the authoritative availability report, so optional absence must remain a capability/validation condition rather than being inferred from catalog membership.
5. `AwaitJobLive` submits the serialized graph and consumes its socket stream. Progress and previews become T2I progress/media callbacks; final binary or history-referenced outputs are loaded into Swarm media objects; cancellation removes the queued prompt, interrupts it when active, and deletes history. The surrounding engine/output flow then applies metadata, persistence, streamed result, and history handoff as described above. The current debug/verbose logs can include the fully substituted graph (F25).
6. Direct Comfy UI traffic instead passes through `/ComfyBackendDirect`: permission checks select one or multiple direct/remote backends, `ComfyUser` maps client/prompt IDs and claims, WebSockets relay status/binary frames, and HTTP prompt/queue/interrupt/view/object-info traffic is rewritten or proxied. Cached object-info merges known backend schemas for the UI; F24 is the bounded warm-fallback correction in that merge owner, implemented and maintainer-validated with environment and backend topology not provided. Install/update routes are a distinct administrative flow and do not run during normal generation.

## Completed-Refactor Assessment

### Image Editing UI coordinator extraction — completed

Current base source matches the extraction's intended boundary. The coordinator state and implementation live in `helpers/image_editor_ui.js`; `currentimagehandler.js` retains only the bounded lazy-loader dispatch needed by its maintained image actions; base `main.js` owns construction of the distinct Generate-tab editor and calls the coordinator only from the Image Editing lazy hook; and `ImageEditingTab.cshtml` handlers resolve to coordinator functions or shared slider styling. The Text2Image manifest orders color picker, tool classes, editor classes, then coordinator, and both first-open Image Editing and first-use Generate editing go through that same group. Activation/deactivation and layer transfer include explicit error display and handler cleanup.

Disposition: **completed**, with static design conformance. No new source evidence reopens the extraction. The retained classic globals, `window.imageEditor`, inline handlers, current-image loader facade, DOM IDs, local-storage keys, and ImageEditor class dependencies are compatibility surfaces, not duplicate implementations. In particular, `layout.js` must continue to preserve Generate-editor/current-image sizing, resizing, and splitter geometry, while `params.js` must preserve width/height synchronization, editor-driven parameter visibility, and image/mask/dimension generation input. Live editor switching, tool behavior, state persistence, those direct-consumer behaviors, and layer transfer remain maintainer-validation boundaries because the audit did not run a browser.

### Image-history controller and collaborators — completed

Current base source has one `ImageHistoryController` construction and one owner for browser, request, refresh, selection, busy, and media-registration state. `ImageHistoryFilter`, `ImageHistoryComparison`, and `ImageHistoryBulkActions` own their designed state and receive narrow service callbacks. Global functions after composition are delegates; `imageHistoryBrowser` is a getter for the controller's exact browser; `registeredMediaButtons` is a getter for its exact registry. Maintained generation/current-image/Grid Generator notification consumers still resolve through `notifyImageHistorySavedPath`, and current-image navigation/button consumers remain supported.

Disposition: **completed**, with static design conformance. The compatibility accessors are still actively consumed and cannot be removed as cleanup. No collaborator directly owns controller request/selection state, bulk work uses selection snapshots and `finally`, and the request lifecycle has explicit stale-response and bounded-retry controls. The full-list and visible-window costs below are measurement seams, not evidence that the ownership refactor should be reopened. Cold/history-open loading, mutation behavior, comparison, filtering, partial failure, and generation-time insertion remain maintainer-validation boundaries.

### T2I API capability split — completed with intentional compatibility bridges

Current source and extraction history (`d13c2b5a`, `d65159d8`, and `ac6b207e`) match the approved split. `BasicAPIFeatures.Register` still calls only `T2IAPI.Register`; that method preserves the 21-route sequence, names, user-update flags, and permission objects while registering implementation delegates from `ImageHistoryAPI`, `KritaAPI`, and `ClassicInpaintAPI`. The registered methods retain their API descriptions, parameter names/types/defaults, and socket/non-socket shapes, so reflection binding and generated documentation now point at the capability owners without changing the wire contract. `ImageHistoryAPI` is the sole history implementation owner, `KritaAPI` the sole bridge owner, and `ClassicInpaintAPI` the sole process/image-operation owner.

The remaining `T2IAPI` methods are compatibility forwarders required by the split design: history `AddImageToHistory`, `ListImages`, `RescanImageMetadata`, `OpenImageFolder`, `DeleteImage`, `BulkMoveImages`, `ToggleImageStarred`, `ToggleImageHidden`, `SetImageRating`, `SetImageTags`, and `SetImageNotes`; Krita `SendImageToKrita`, `ImportKritaImage`, `CheckPendingKritaImage`, and `GetActiveKritaSession`; and classic-inpaint `GetIOPaintCommandCandidates`, `RunProcessCapture`, `PrepareClassicInpaintMask`, `GetSupportedClassicInpaintBackends`, `GetClassicInpaintBackends`, and `ClassicInpaint`. `BackendAPI` has three maintained calls through `T2IAPI.RunProcessCapture`. Grid Generator's `history/` data-delete branch passes `Grids/{...}/swarm_save_config.json` to `T2IAPI.DeleteImage`, retaining the forwarding dependency while deleting the saved grid configuration at its actual output-relative path.

Generation-owned `T2IAPI.RequestToParams` has five maintained caller contexts: core `GenT2I_Internal` converts a generation request; `ImageHistoryAPI.AddImageToHistory` derives metadata for a supplied image without queueing generation; `ComfyUIWebAPI.ComfyGetGeneratedWorkflow` validates input before producing workflow JSON; Grid Generator `GridGenRun` converts its `baseParams` with preset retention; and Image Batch `GenBatchRun_Internal` converts shared `baseParams` before per-file generation. `UtilAPI` intentionally adjusts `T2IAPI.LastRefreshed`, and Comfy contributes to `T2IAPI.AlwaysTopKeys`.

Five legacy history data contracts also intentionally remain authoritative in `T2IAPI`: `HistoryExtensions`, `ImageHistorySortMode`, `ImageHistoryHelper`, `ImageHistoryIndexWarmups`, and `DeletableFileExtensions`. `ImageHistoryAPI` qualifies these rather than duplicating them, preserving nested type names and mutable field/object identity for external extensions. Maintained source has no additional caller that justifies migrating these contracts now.

Disposition: **completed with intentional compatibility bridges**, not an incomplete ownership extraction. Static source confirms one implementation per moved capability and the exact maintained facade consumers above. Removing or relocating the forwarders/data contracts would be a separate compatibility-breaking project with no current reliability or measured-performance payoff, so this refresh does not reopen it. Maintainer validation remains the original route matrix: HTTP/WebSocket generation and direct history insertion; history listing/mutations/Grid cleanup; Krita loopback/forwarding behavior; classic-inpaint discovery/process failure; and unchanged permissions, reflection metadata, errors, cancellation, and JSON shapes.

### Comfy capability, class-name, and input-name catalogs — completed

Current base source matches the two catalog designs and their staged implementation history (`77ceed11` through `c889a04d` for class names/capabilities and `d5d37367` through `5994853f` plus the subsequent consumer commits for input names). `ComfyNodeNames` has exactly 65 documented constants matching the completed maintained Python registration inventory. `ComfyNodeInputNames` has exactly 46 node-scoped classes and 184 documented schema-backed constants for the keys consumed by built-in C#. `ComfyCapabilityCatalog.CreateNodeToFeatureMap` retains the existing mapping pairs, including literal third-party names, and returns the mutable dictionary assigned to the public `ComfyUIBackendExtension.NodeToFeatureMap` field.

All maintained in-scope class-name consumers now use the catalog across `ComfyCapabilityCatalog`, `ComfyUIAPIAbstractBackend`, `ComfyUIBackendExtension`, `ComfyUIWebAPI`, `WGNodeData`, `WorkflowGenerator`, `WorkflowGeneratorModelSupport`, and `WorkflowGeneratorSteps`; input-name consumers use the latter seven files. The input catalog deliberately excludes Python-only inputs, upstream/third-party inputs, envelope keys, and five documented compatibility extras. Optional ClipSeg and Extra-package mappings remain conditional Python imports, and neither catalog claims they are installed. Python mappings/schemas and backend `object_info` therefore remain runtime authority exactly as designed.

Disposition: **completed**, with static design conformance and no cosmetic follow-up. F22's implemented boundary concerns where per-backend discovery results are stored and advertised, not the catalog values or `NodeToFeatureMap` compatibility surface; F25 concerns logging after substitution, not the input catalog. F26 uses the catalogs as prerequisites but preserves them unchanged. Live validation remains the original common-only/optional-node matrix across object-info, still/mask/SAM/segmentation, model helpers, audio/video/animation, direct LoRA extraction, and useful missing-node errors.

## Evidence-Backed Findings

### Frontend F1 — historical pre-Rank-18 WebSocket invalid-session retry dropped caller lifecycle handlers

- **Historical boundary and exact pre-Rank-18 evidence:** at the approved base, `site.js::makeWSRequest(url, in_data, callback, depth, errorHandle, onOpenHandle)` used the supplied `errorHandle` for initial failures and called `onOpenHandle` after the initial socket opened. On an `invalid_session_id` message, lines 130–138 at that base called `getSession` and then `makeWSRequest(url, in_data, callback, depth + 1)` without either handler. The only maintained `onOpenHandle` consumer is `ModelDownloaderUtil.download` in `utiltab.js`: it binds the cancel button to send a cancel signal through the opened socket and then enables that button.
- **Known maintained consumers:** `generatecontrols.js::makeWSRequestT2I` passes generation error handlers; `GenerateHandler.doGenerate` depends on that handler to clear socket/tracked-card state and report/retry previews. `models.js::trt_modal_create`, Utilities' LoRA extraction and model download flows, and `installer.js` also call `makeWSRequest`; the model and Utilities flows pass UI-restoring error handlers. The model downloader additionally supplies the sole maintained open handler for cancellation. At the approved base, the installer disabled `installer_button_confirm` before its request and restored it only in the supplied error handler, so a failed invalid-session retry could leave that button disabled. External classic-script consumers cannot be inventoried and are a preservation constraint.
- **Historical control flow at the approved base:** the first socket preserved caller callbacks. If the server rejected its session, the transport reacquired a session and created a second socket. Errors on that retry took the generic `showError` path, and its open event could not invoke the original `onOpenHandle`; the downloader's cancel button therefore remained bound to the initially opened, rejected socket rather than the retry socket.
- **Historical problem/risk and certainty:** a retry after session renewal changed the request's error/open lifecycle contract. For generation this could bypass the handler that cleared the tracked socket/cards; for TensorRT or Utilities it could bypass button/progress restoration; for the installer it could leave the confirm button disabled; and after a successful model-download renewal, cancellation remained bound to the initially rejected socket instead of the active retry socket. The argument loss and changed branch were **statically confirmed**; how often an invalid session reached either a successful retry needing cancellation or a later retry error was runtime-dependent.

The sensitive concrete consumer is `usertab.js`; external callers remain a compatibility constraint.
- **Current control flow and implementation state:** **Implemented, awaiting maintainer validation.** The client no longer serializes `in_data`; network/timeout failures still use `describeRequestFailure`, callers with an `errorHandle` still receive the error, and endpoint-generated `data.error` remains unchanged and may intentionally contain a submitted value for context—for example, Image History returns `Invalid sort mode '{sortBy}'.`
- **Problem/risk and certainty:** the prior client-added disclosure path was **statically confirmed**. Its removal is statically visible, but browser console behavior, callback/UI parity, and representative sensitive/large error flows still require maintainer validation; no confidentiality claim is made about endpoint-generated `data.error` text.

Auditing or redacting endpoint-generated server errors is a separate project and out of scope.
- **Bounded implementation and rollback:** the client-added `JSON.stringify(in_data)` diagnostic is removed without a replacement that serializes request values; `data.error` is not altered. Rollback may restore endpoint/failure identity through a value-free structural statement, but must not restore arbitrary request serialization.
- **Verification and validation:** static verification searches client request-payload logs and representative sensitive/large callers, proves the server-provided error still reaches the same log/UI/callback path unchanged, and confirms no client diagnostic serializes/adds `in_data` values. ### Core F5 — streaming WebSocket producer faults are transported after queued frames drain

- **Boundary and exact evidence:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** The approved design is commit `47d32f4e`; the exact production range is `5511d3a6^..84df8dfe` (parent `51268f31` through `84df8dfe`) and contains source commits `5511d3a6`, `f5f041b4`, `a1e32947`, `f02bb90d`, and `84df8dfe`. It changes exactly `src/WebAPI/API.cs`, `src/WebAPI/ModelsAPI.cs`, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIWebAPI.cs`, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ImageBatchTool/ImageBatchToolExtension.cs`, and `src/WebAPI/T2IAPI.cs`. The inventory remains exactly one `RunWebsocketHandlerCallWS<T>` definition and six calls: initial and socket-reuse T2I, Models, TensorRT, LoRA, and Image Batch.
- **Known maintained consumers:** `T2IAPI.GenerateText2ImageWS`, `ModelsAPI.SelectModelWS`, Comfy `DoLoraExtractionWS` and `DoTensorRTCreateWS`, and Image Batch Tool `ImageBatchRun` remain the five route owners. Browser consumers remain generation controls, model selection, Utilities LoRA/TensorRT progress, and Image Batch Tool; no browser file, route registration, public route signature, permission, request parameter, producer signature, dispatcher contract, or direct-helper contract changed.
- **Current control flow:** `RunWebsocketHandlerCallWS<T>` now returns `Task<bool>`. It drains the producer's queued frames first; after producer completion and queue exhaustion, `t.IsFaulted` causes the detailed readable exception to be logged, one `Utilities.ErrorObj("An internal error occurred", "internal_error")` frame to be sent, and `false` to be returned. Normal or canceled producer completion returns `true`. Explicit producer-enqueued readable errors are transported without interpretation, and socket-send exceptions escape. Models branches before final status; LoRA before refresh/output inspection/terminal logs and frames; Image Batch before its success log/frame; and TensorRT returns without changing its successful producer-internal refresh/completion path.
- **T2I coordination:** both calls and the tracked set use `Task<bool>`. The route distinguishes `producerFailed` from `helperTaskFailed`: a successful task returning `false` stops receive/reuse acceptance, while a faulted or canceled helper task retains the first captured helper-task exception with `ExceptionDispatchInfo`. Both states cancel the receive wait and suppress advisory close/final status. Receive checks before and after input and immediately before helper creation bound the race. Already-started helpers remain tracked and are all observed and removed; only after the active set drains is the first captured helper-task exception rethrown. Successful socket reuse, batch offsets, two-second reuse window, advisory close intention, final status, and dispatcher-owned closure remain in the existing flow.
- **Problem/risk and certainty:** the prior swallowed task-fault state and contradictory Image Batch success continuation were **statically confirmed**. The new failure transport and wrapper branches are statically confirmed in the fixed production range. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the complete runtime matrix on Linux on 2026-07-25. Windows and other-platform behavior, producer fault frequency, and performance remain unvalidated.
- **Compatibility constraints and caveats:** producer-enqueued readable errors remain semantic producer output rather than helper failure. A legacy Image Batch producer can therefore enqueue a readable error, complete normally, and reach the pre-existing wrapper success continuation; that behavior is unchanged and outside this unexpected-task-fault project. The helper return-type change is source-compatible for the maintained calls but is not claimed to preserve arbitrary precompiled binary ABI. `ExtensionsManager.BuildExtension` includes extension source identity plus current core version and core module MVID in its managed extension cache target, providing recompilation evidence for that maintained source-extension path, not a guarantee for independently precompiled binaries.
- **Verification and validation:** static review confirmed the exact five commits/five files, one-definition/six-call inventory, post-drain detailed-log/generic-frame/`false` ordering, uninterpreted readable errors, canceled-producer `true`, escaping sends, all wrapper branches, T2I receive/race/drain/drain-before-rethrow flow, unchanged true paths, unchanged direct helper/dispatcher/routes/browser surface, core-MVID cache identity, and clean fixed-range whitespace. On 2026-07-25, maintainer Reaper176 confirmed on Linux all six injected-fault flows; ordered progress, one generic failure, server-only detail, contradictory-finalization suppression, normal closure, and no duplicates; the T2I receive/active-drain/one-way-state/race matrix; preserved readable-error, cancellation, send-failure/remote-disconnect, route/permission/browser-cleanup, and client-data-security behavior; and all six successful-flow categories. No agent build, test, launcher, server, socket, browser, backend, Comfy, performance, platform runtime exercise, or other runtime validation occurred.

### Core F6 — server-settings mutation and persistence are not one observable transaction

- **Boundary and exact evidence:** at the pinned audit base, `AdminAPI.ChangeServerSettings` mutated accepted fields in `Program.ServerSettings`, called void `Program.SaveSettingsFile`, and only afterward validated model-path directories; path rejection restored only `Paths`, while `settings_editor.js::save_server_settings` retained the complete altered set on an error response. `SaveSettingsFile` also caught/logged filesystem errors without returning an outcome. The bounded repair is implemented by original production commits `37ae1982`, `761b101d`, `77e1ea66`, `ca6471a9`, `ac113e38`, and `0b31b59f`, plus final-review corrections `6dd92eed`, `6cc4a547`, `f13b1da7`, `9eba70d5`, and `4b54241a`, in `src/Core/Program.cs`, `src/WebAPI/AdminAPI.cs`, `src/Core/Installation.cs`, `src/WebAPI/BackendAPI.cs`, and `src/WebAPI/ClassicInpaintAPI.cs`.
- **Known maintained consumers:** the route remains registered as HTTP `ChangeServerSettings`, user-update `true`, with `Permissions.EditServerSettings`; the server settings editor remains its maintained browser caller. Installation completion, Admin extension operations, Backend API IOPaint settings/install/uninstall/status paths, and Classic Inpaint capability and execution paths now participate in the corrected persistence/lifecycle ownership.
- **Current control flow and implementation state:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** `Program` assigns `--lock_settings` before load/normalization and exposes saved/locked/failed outcomes behind one maintained transaction owner while retaining the void compatibility facade. `Saved` means the authoritative file contains the intended serialization: a `SaveToFile` exception is verified against that file to distinguish committed journal-cleanup failure from true persistence failure, while `always_pull` remains derived. `ChangeServerSettings` filters into an isolated candidate without tentative shared callbacks, applies the canonical `userauthorization.authorizationrequired` lockout guard and case-insensitive path trigger, validates and persists before publication, then guards committed callbacks and runtime actions under the fixed success-warning contract. Extension and IOPaint mutations restore affected snapshots on persistence failure. Installer choices remain validation-only before backend work and merge into a fresh latest-live candidate at final save. One async IOPaint lifecycle semaphore covers dedicated and generic settings mutations, status, capability probing, and active Classic Inpaint execution using immutable inputs across awaits and lifecycle-before-settings lock order; no destructive compensation was added. On 2026-07-23, maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the normal build/launch workflow and full thirteen-case matrix. Core F7 remains the independent model-catalog coordination project.
- **Problem/risk and certainty:** the prior partial rejection and false durable-success paths were **statically confirmed**. Fresh whole-project specification review and final quality re-review approved the corrected production state. Maintainer Reaper176 subsequently confirmed the normal build/launch workflow and the named filesystem-failure, concurrency, editor/restart-agreement, runtime-side-effect, lifecycle-overlap, and marker matrix.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve AutoConfiguration field names/types/metadata, secret placeholder behavior, hidden-field rejection, permissions, `ChangeServerSettings` request/response names, settings-file format/path, legacy migrations, lock-settings semantics, launcher `always_pull` marker, and runtime side effects. User settings and role/backend stores already centralize persistence under their owning locks; at the pinned audit base, the model-path branch's limited `Paths` snapshot demonstrated the local compensation pattern but did not protect mixed requests.
- **Bounded implementation and rollback:** the observable owner preserves the settings format and legacy facade; candidate publication fixes `ChangeServerSettings`, affected snapshots contain extension/IOPaint failures, the fresh installation candidate avoids stale rollback, and the lifecycle semaphore protects IOPaint mutations and consumers without destructive compensation. Rollback may retain the observable owner wherever a migrated caller depends on it and must not restore false durable-success responses, partial candidate publication, tentative callbacks, stale installation rollback, or unlocked active IOPaint consumers.
- **Verification and validation:** static verification traces the observable owner, authoritative-file verification, early lock flag, compatibility facade, maintained callers, candidate filtering/guard/validation/save/publication/committed-callback sequence, extension and IOPaint rollback paths, fresh installation merge, IOPaint lifecycle ordering and immutable await inputs, marker classification, client callback, and Core F7 separation. On 2026-07-23, maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the normal build/launch workflow and approved thirteen-case settings transaction/persistence matrix.

### Core F7 — runtime model-path edits replace the shared catalog outside its refresh boundary

No read claim upgrades to a write claim or spans network/download, child-process, WebSocket, or backend/GPU work merely to retain a copied destination. - **Implemented lifecycle under static inspection:** maintained issuance and consumption reconcile metadata, invalidate ages of 15 minutes or more, and finish at no more than 256 entries. Expiry precedes live eviction; deterministic oldest selection uses creation tick and ordinal tracker tie-breaking; only the newest tracker for one email remains; metadata-free legacy entries are reconciled when first encountered; issuance retries unique `TryAdd`; and consumption is atomic, one-way, and never restored after later account/link work.
- **Preserved contracts:** the public `TempAuths` field ABI, type, mutability, and identity; `CheckOAuth(string)` signature and tuple meaning; username validation and both rate limits before consumption; API route/signature/sessionless behavior; browser key/parameter/tracker format; Google verification and registered-login flow; error IDs/UI behavior; logs and sensitive-data exposure; settings timing; account creation/linking and `SetOAuthEmail` final authority; persistence; and unrelated public members remain unchanged. The dedicated lock covers only in-memory challenge bookkeeping, with no network, database, account, response, or logging work under it and no new email/tracker logging.

### Core F9 — Google OAuth login cookie transport flags differed from password login

- **Boundary and exact evidence:** on an already registered OAuth mapping, `GoogleOAuthVerify.cshtml` creates a persistent login session and appends `swarm_token`. The writer now sets `Secure = HttpContext.Request.IsHttps`, exactly matching the request-aware decision used by password `Login` and `Logout`. `WebUtil.GetValidLogin` accepts the cookie and validates its persistent token for protected Razor/output/API-session creation flows.
- **Known maintained consumers:** the Google OAuth callback page is the sole maintained OAuth-login cookie writer; password `Login` is the comparison writer. `WebServer.GetUserFor`, `GeneratePageModel`, `GetNewSession`, output/audio/special routes, and other authorization-gated page flows consume the cookie through `WebUtil`.
- **Current control flow and implementation state:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** A verified known email creates a new hashed persistent login token in LiteDB, sends the plaintext token in the browser cookie with the request-aware `Secure` decision, and redirects to Text2Image. Cookie name/value, HttpOnly, one-year expiry, SameSite, session creation/storage/validation, redirect, OAuth registration, readers, and protected consumers are unchanged. No forwarded-header, trusted-proxy, Kestrel, settings, launcher, or Web-pipeline behavior changed.
- **Problem/risk and certainty:** before implementation, the OAuth path weakened the transport flag of the long-lived credential relative to password login, allowing a browser to attach it to a later HTTP request to the same host when such a path was reachable. The prior omitted flag and the current writer alignment are **statically confirmed**. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the agreed HTTP OAuth login/logout and session-usability, direct HTTPS OAuth Secure-cookie/session/logout, applicable HTTP/HTTPS password login/logout compatibility, supported HTTPS proxy behavior based on the scheme Swarm observes, OAuth registration/redirect, and protected-page/API-session matrix; no benchmark or performance measurement is claimed.
- **Compatibility constraints and deployment boundary:** Direct HTTPS, or trusted hosting that already establishes the ASP.NET request scheme as HTTPS, produces a Secure OAuth cookie. A TLS-terminating proxy whose Swarm-facing request remains HTTP is a separate scheme-establishment limitation; this change does not trust raw forwarding headers. Preserve localhost/reverse-proxy deployments and password/OAuth interoperability.
- **Implemented boundary and rollback:** only the OAuth writer's secure decision was aligned. Token format, hashing, expiry, SameSite, redirect, OAuth registration, neighboring authentication behavior, and proxy scheme establishment were not redesigned. Rollback is isolated to the cookie option.
- **Verification and validation:** static verification compares all `swarm_token` append/clear sites and login readers. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the named HTTP, direct HTTPS, supported-proxy, OAuth/password login/logout, registration/redirect, and protected-consumer matrix. The validation proves the agreed behavior; trusted proxy scheme establishment remains separately scoped and raw forwarding headers remain untrusted and out of scope.

### Core F10 — shutdown idempotence used a non-atomic check/set across concurrent entry points

- **Implementation status and boundaries:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.** The approved design base is `3d244ede73a3cc36343624bdd00329a8a5284e5b`. The integrated history through production head `f389da0d13be6bbf4cc81f6c9a24c21a095cdaca` contains documentation-plan commit `8083c644a37ff614099f5f105e87af091cae0615` and production commit `f389da0d13be6bbf4cc81f6c9a24c21a095cdaca`; the production path-filtered history contains only the latter. Production changes exactly `src/Core/Program.cs`, with `3 insertions(+), 3 deletions(-)`.
- **Original boundary and exact evidence:** at the pinned audit/design boundary, `Program.Shutdown` checked `private volatile static bool HasShutdown`, returned if true, then assigned true with separate operations. Maintained callers were assembly unload, process exit, ASP.NET `ApplicationStopping`, the main thread after `WebApp.WaitForShutdown`, `RequestRestart`, the control-timeout tick, CI shutdown, and permission-gated `AdminAPI.ShutdownServer`, which returned success while scheduling a half-second-delayed direct shutdown. Several could originate on framework/background threads.
- **Known maintained consumers:** every process-wide owner in the shutdown sequence depends on one entry: shutdown webhook, `PreShutdownEvent` (AutoScalingBackend), `GlobalCancelSource`, `WebServer.WebApp`, `BackendHandler`, `SessionHandler`, proxy, all `T2IModelHandler` instances, extension `OnShutdown`, `OutputMetadataTracker`, temp deletion, and log flushing.
- **Original control flow, problem, and certainty:** the former guard was evaluated before a bounded webhook wait and before cancellation/disposal. Two concurrent callers could both read false before either wrote true and then execute the same ordered sequence against shared owners. Individual owners had mixed idempotence: extensions were later cleared, session/backend handlers had their own Boolean guards, model handlers had instance guards, while webhook, pre-shutdown event, Web stop, metadata shutdown, temp removal, and log flushing were composition-level operations. The former check/set race and multiple thread-capable entry points were **statically confirmed**; actual simultaneous entry was not observed and its frequency was unknown.
- **Current behavior:** the XML-documented private static integer gate is now the first `Shutdown` operation. `Interlocked.CompareExchange(ref HasShutdown, 1, 0)` gives the zero-to-one winner exclusive entry and every loser returns immediately before the webhook, exit-code handling, event, cancellation, disposal, cleanup, or logs. A winning code `42` retains its existing assignment; a winning zero makes no assignment and preserves any prior CI exit code; losers cannot alter the code. The complete post-gate body, order, waits, and failure behavior are unchanged, and a winner failure does not reopen the gate for retry or repair.
- **Compatibility and ABI boundary:** direct and restart caller inventories and signatures are unchanged. `ShutdownServer` permission, early-success response, half-second delay, code `42`, pre-cancel event order, global cancellation timing, owner order, bounded webhook wait, extension isolation, and final log flush remain unchanged. `HasShutdown` is private, so the storage change does not alter the public source or binary extension ABI. Rank 13 does not redesign asynchronous shutdown or Backend F18/rank 14 task ownership.
- **Static review, maintainer validation, and caveats:** fixed-range log, source diff, numstat, caller/signature search, literal-code, post-gate-body, and `git diff --check` review confirmed the distinct integrated/production histories, one-file `3/3` source scope, gate placement, first-caller semantics, and preserved contracts. Independent source reviews returned `SOURCE_SPEC_APPROVED` and `SOURCE_QUALITY_APPROVED`, both with no issues. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 explicitly confirmed all 16 exact cases passed on 2026-07-27 on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs: ordinary/direct/admin shutdown, uncontested restart, both ordinary/restart winner outcomes, prior CI-code preservation, Web/admin/restart/control-timeout collisions, later unload/exit callbacks, webhook bound and order, full owner order, per-extension isolation, and the non-resetting failure boundary. Agents performed no build, test, launch, runtime, platform, or performance exercise. Windows, other Linux distributions/filesystems, and performance remain unvalidated or unmeasured; rollback is the one local guard implementation.

### Core F11 — the shared download helper did not fully honor replacement or caller cancellation

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** The production implementation is exactly `24157f35 fix: truncate shared download replacements` and `051a5bb5 fix: cancel shared download requests`, the inclusive range `24157f35^..051a5bb5`, changing only `src/Utils/Utilities.cs`. The fixed design boundary is `23c77542`; `93e1a50c`, `9558e289`, `75c93bda`, and `329b7cce` are documentation-only plan/lifetime corrections rather than production.
- **Boundary and original evidence:** before those production commits, `Utilities.DownloadFile` opened the destination with `File.OpenWrite(filepath)`, which wrote from position zero without truncating an existing longer file. Its initial and range-retry `UtilWebClient.SendAsync` calls passed only `Program.GlobalProgramCancel`, even though the method created `combinedCancel` from that token and its caller-supplied `CancellationTokenSource`; caller cancellation reached stream reads/writes and polling only after response headers. Hash verification covered enqueued downloaded chunks, not any stale trailing destination bytes.
- **Known maintained consumers:** seven direct calls exist: four fixed-path `Installation` calls download Comfy archives and the VC redistributable; `CommonModels.ModelInfo.DownloadNow` downloads a registered common model with its expected hash; `WorkflowGenerator.DownloadModel` downloads a required model to a pre-deleted `.tmp` file with an expected hash; and `ModelsAPI.DoModelDownloadWS` downloads to a pre-deleted `.download.tmp` path, supplies the only maintained caller cancellation source, and receives cancel signals from the model-downloader WebSocket. `ModelInfo.DownloadNow` has three maintained indirect callers: `Installation.Models` and the auto-VAE and LTX audio-VAE branches in `WorkflowGeneratorModelSupport`; it checks the model-handler destination and logs/returns rather than downloading when that destination already exists.
- **Original control flow:** the helper created the directory/file, sent the request, started load/save/progress tasks, buffered chunks, supported range retries, verified content length and optional SHA-256, deleted on known-length mismatch, hash mismatch, or writer failure, and awaited all three tasks; unknown-length producer failure or cancellation could leave a partial destination because the length check was skipped. Installation's model-selection flow awaited `ModelInfo.DownloadNow` with progress/error reporting. The two Comfy model-support paths synchronously waited for the same owner when their known VAE was absent, then called `RefreshAllModelSets`; this was distinct from `WorkflowGenerator.DownloadModel`, which pre-deleted its own temporary target before its direct helper call. A model-UI cancel before headers set the caller source but could not cancel the active send; an existing longer target in a direct caller without a fresh-target precondition could retain trailing bytes after an otherwise length/hash-valid transfer.
- **Original problem/risk and certainty:** a direct caller that did not establish a fresh-target precondition could produce a corrupt on-disk artifact not represented by the verified chunk hash, and early model-UI cancellation could remain pending until the request headers arrived or the client timeout/failure occurred. Both token selection and non-truncating open mode were **statically confirmed**; encountering such a longer direct target or slow/stalled headers was runtime-dependent.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve download URLs/headers, progress callback timing/fields, five-minute no-data handling, four range retries, helper deletion on known-length mismatch, hash mismatch, and writer failure, the unknown-length partial-file gap, expected-hash behavior, global shutdown cancellation, fixed installer paths, and WebSocket cancel semantics. Preserve `ModelInfo.DownloadNow`'s known-model destination mapping, pre-existing-file refusal, expected hash, installation progress/error behavior, and synchronous auto-VAE/LTX audio-VAE download-then-refresh behavior. Models API and `WorkflowGenerator.DownloadModel` already delete their distinct temporary targets before starting, while only `WorkflowGenerator.DownloadModel` separately owns catch cleanup.
- **Implemented boundary and preserved contracts:** the accepted-response order is linked-token initial send, `OK` validation, linked-token stream acquisition, destination `FileMode.Create`, then producer/writer/progress workers. The same linked caller/global token reaches initial and retry sends, stream acquisition, reads, writes, and polling. The linked source is disposed; caller-owned sources are not disposed; and the helper-created default source from `cancel ??= new()` remains legacy-undisposed. The public signature, callers, URLs/headers, four-retry and `PartialContent` policy, buffering, progress, content-length/SHA-256 checks, logging, destinations, moves, refreshes, and global shutdown participation remain unchanged. Known-length mismatch, hash mismatch, and writer failure retain deletion, while unknown-length producer failure or cancellation may leave a partial destination because the length check is skipped. Adjacent legacy behavior also remains: unknown-length 1024-byte buffering can truncate the download to 1024 bytes while returning false success; retry `Range(totalRead, length)` remains literal and inclusive without `Content-Range` validation; terminal progress precedes validation; sibling tasks are not explicitly canceled on a peer failure; and SHA-256 remains undisposed.
- **Static verification and validation:** repeated searches found all seven direct helper calls and three indirect `ModelInfo.DownloadNow` flows; `git diff --name-only 24157f35^..051a5bb5` returned only `src/Utils/Utilities.cs`; fixed-range log/diff inspection separated the two production commits from documentation; source traces confirmed ordering, token propagation, preserved policy/callers, the known-length/hash/writer deletion paths, the unknown-length partial-file gap, and cancellation-source lifetimes; and `git diff --check 24157f35^..051a5bb5` reported no whitespace errors. Protected maintainer work and the protected backup were excluded. On 2026-07-25, maintainer Reaper176 confirmed on Linux that all 14 approved cases passed: exact replacement of a longer target; pre-header caller cancellation without destination mutation; known-length midstream cancellation with established cleanup while preserving the unknown-length partial-file gap; global shutdown cancellation; successful and failed/non-partial range continuation; content-length and SHA-256 failure cleanup; fixed installer downloads; installation common-model downloads; model UI success, progress, cancellation, target, move, refresh, and cleanup behavior; `WorkflowGenerator.DownloadModel` success and cleanup; both Comfy model-support download/refresh paths; and ordinary success/progress behavior. The adjacent unknown-length 1024-byte truncation/false-success behavior remains uncorrected and is not claimed fixed. Windows runtime behavior and performance remain unvalidated. No agent runtime validation or platform/performance claim is made.

### Core F12 — model metadata cache reuse does not track sidecar freshness

- **Implementation status and provenance:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Firefox (version not provided).** Approved source/audit base `d2508564975c5ca149048e29f57e428dde6d96f2`, design `2048a2bb67e9c8da31233f729695e5d7469683fb`, plan `e68ffce03535ff8d5948226e4a23d0c062a8cce6`, production `207c595c01eefd27987159b770fc99ca7b26ae0d`, post-production precision/quality correction `ccb016228e071fc06ce09b364b7796be98511da3`, post-implementation record `77f8cf47d44afd6ebe94b1cbeb3e645ce5808db1`, and implementation-record quality correction `bd81cdaf15bcc3efb4d40519161a7c30ec8461f8` form the integrated history. The exact source projection changes only `src/Text2Image/T2IModelHandler.cs`, with `29 insertions(+), 2 deletions(-)` and resulting blob `1db9a1d609911cbfc98277904ca481635aa52aa5`; source is unchanged after production.
- **Historical boundary and exact evidence:** at the approved base, `T2IModelHandler.LoadMetadata` selected the central or per-folder LiteDB database, read `ModelMetadataStore` by raw model path or folder-relative filename, invalidated a legacy variable-text-encoder record when `TextEncoders` was null, and otherwise recomputed only when `metadata.ModelFileVersion` differed from the model file's last-write timestamp. The cache record had no sidecar fingerprint. Sidecars were and remain first-class inputs: `AltModelMetadataJsonFileSuffixes` is the ordered set `.swarm.json`, `.json`, `.cm-info.json`, and `.civitai.info`; recomputation checks existence, reads, parses, merges, and extracts metadata from them.
- **Maintained consumers and refresh entry points:** cached/sidecar-derived `T2IModel` fields feed Models API describe/list/edit responses, `T2IAPI.ListT2IParams`, model validation/metadata and prompt trigger handling, Comfy workflow/model support, remote-backend model serialization, and special model viewing. Refresh reaches `LoadMetadata` at startup through `Program.RefreshAllModelSets`; through `T2IAPI.TriggerRefresh` invoking `ModelRefreshEvent` (each handler subscribes); after path edits in `AdminAPI`; after TensorRT creation and the two known-model VAE downloads through `RefreshAllModelSets`; and through direct handler refresh after model download, LoRA extraction, and installation model setup. These are the complete maintained refresh call categories found at the pinned tree.
- **Implemented data/control flow:** the additive public persistence property `ModelMetadataStore.ModelSidecarFingerprint` stores exactly four ordered entries for `.swarm.json`, `.json`, `.cm-info.json`, and `.civitai.info`; each entry is either an explicit missing marker or invariant file length plus UTC last-write ticks. `LoadMetadata` captures the selected model prefix's fingerprint once before cache lookup and reuses a record only when it exists, the legacy condition does not invalidate it, the model-file timestamp matches, and the non-null stored fingerprint matches. Legacy null records and ordinary supported-sidecar add/edit/delete changes recompute. The same conservative pre-read capture is stored on the newly constructed record, so a concurrent later sidecar change forces another future recomputation rather than being falsely recorded as consumed. The unchanged recomputation still owns the two ordered sidecar read/parse passes, merge, `procAltHeader`, limits, class inference, image validation, and publication.
- **Maintained sidecar/edit contracts and writer semantics:** `T2IModel.ResaveModel` still writes `.swarm.json` for non-safetensors/non-SFT models or when `Metadata.EditMetadataWriteJSON` is enabled; ordered suffix handling supplies fallback metadata, and `AllModelAttachedExtensions` still treats preview and metadata files as attachments. `ResetMetadataFrom` captures the same fingerprint before its own lock statements, mutates the in-memory record, and then attempts the unchanged central/per-folder upsert. A caught `LoadMetadata` upsert failure can leave the prior durable record while the newly constructed metadata/fingerprint still publishes in memory, but existing `HadNewError` threshold handling may instead dispose/delete that database; a later refresh reevaluates a retained old record or rebuilds after cache reset. `ResetMetadataFrom` retains its log-and-throw boundary after the pre-upsert in-memory mutation. Maintained hash/resave callers may already hold the reentrant `ModificationLock`, so the textual capture position is not a universal outside-lock guarantee. Edit/bulk-edit deferred resave timing, download-provided sidecars, duplicate propagation, cache IDs, settings, permissions, responses, and all consumers remain unchanged.
- **Historical problem/risk and certainty:** at the approved base, a normal refresh was presented as rescanning file-backed model metadata, but a supported attachment could change without invalidating its cached contribution, yielding stale or missing titles, classes, descriptions, trigger phrases, previews/other model metadata until another condition forced recomputation. The former invalidation gap and implemented correction are **statically confirmed**; prior occurrence, duration, and user-facing severity remain unmeasured. The exact unchanged 28-case matrix now has the bounded maintainer runtime record below.
- **Compatibility, non-goals, and rollback:** the optional property keeps old LiteDB records readable and makes their null value recompute once. It is one additive public member on public `ModelMetadataStore`; all pre-existing public members, wire/model JSON and API shapes, central/per-folder database selection and IDs, suffix precedence, invalid-JSON and unreadable-sidecar behavior, edit/download/duplicate writers, refresh entry points, and consumers are otherwise unchanged. Invalid or unreadable changed sidecars enter the existing failure path instead of returning stale data. Rank 22 does not add content hashing, preview freshness, a watcher, a performance change, or permission-only detection; deliberate same-length/same-UTC-time replacement and changes indistinguishable at filesystem timestamp resolution remain outside the bounded guarantee. Rollback can stop consulting the optional field while leaving records readable.
- **Static review and maintainer validation:** design reviews returned `TASK1_SPEC_APPROVED` and `TASK1_QUALITY_APPROVED`; source reviews returned `SOURCE_SPEC_APPROVED` and `SOURCE_QUALITY_APPROVED`; post-implementation documentation reviews returned `TASK3_SPEC_APPROVED` and `TASK3_QUALITY_APPROVED`; validation-document reviews returned `VALIDATION_SPEC_APPROVED` and `VALIDATION_QUALITY_APPROVED`; and review-provenance documentation reviews returned `DOCS_SPEC_APPROVED` and `DOCS_QUALITY_APPROVED`. The first final integrated review returned the missing review-provenance finding; correction `81b1f9b37b6a39fdd779adba2c41700ac72135a5` addressed it. The corrected-state integrated re-review returned `FINAL_INTEGRATED_APPROVED` with no findings. All eleven approvals are current with no findings after the post-production precision/quality, implementation-record quality, and review-provenance corrections. Static checks confirmed the exact projection/blob, four-entry invariant, legacy/null flow, conservative capture, `LoadMetadata` and `ResetMetadataFrom` publication boundaries, lock qualification, central/per-folder behavior, unchanged P4 loops, whitespace, index isolation, and protected work. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 supplied the exact raw message `all passed on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using firefox` on 2026-07-28. Under the controller's disclosed normalization against the exact unchanged 28-case matrix, capitalization is normalized to `All` and `Firefox`, and the outcome is 28 passed, 0 failed, and 0 unrun. No browser version was provided. Cache-mode and model/sidecar arrangement details were not separately provided; all exact matrix cases passed, but no unreported per-case procedure, injected sidecar contents, duplicate topology, or other arrangement detail is attributed to the maintainer. Validation is limited to Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Firefox (version not provided); other platforms, filesystems, browsers, and browser versions remain unvalidated. ### Backend F13 — AutoScalingBackend's historical platform-extension guard was ineffective

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Bash (version not provided); Windows runtime unvalidated.**
- **Historical boundary and exact evidence:** at approved source/audit base `e65550b0b90b62504f0495723db95567fe192673`, `AutoScalingBackend.Init` compared the dotted lowercase result of `Path.GetExtension(Settings.StartScript)` to undotted `"bat"`, `"ps1"`, and `"sh"`, then entered its rejection branch when that allow-list-shaped expression was true. Backend FDS/API configuration supplies `StartScript`, `BackendHandler.LoadBackendDirect` invokes `Init`, and `BackendHandler` registers the type as `autoscalingbackend`; no second maintained extension validator exists.
- **Implemented boundary and provenance:** design `984930bed4624b760b717d06f699e6f653358c28`, plan `58ef998369a51d1c803938c3c89a15c6e472e30b`, plan correction `a107c2834b17a1d715934f71f861c5d05643d3ce`, and production source/head `c82d9b6c8f340c6017349fa01c57788b210735f5` form the integrated Rank 20 history. The exact production projection is only `src/Backends/AutoScalingBackend.cs`, `4 insertions(+), 1 deletion(-)`. Static-only source reviews returned `SOURCE_SPEC_APPROVED` and `SOURCE_QUALITY_APPROVED` with no findings.
- **Implemented truth table and order:** `ToLowerInvariant` makes the final-extension comparison case-insensitive. Windows accepts `.bat` and `.ps1`; every non-Windows platform accepts `.sh`. Empty, trailing-dot, unrelated, and wrong-platform extensions are rejected, and only the final extension participates. Disabled and invalid-settings checks remain before the guard. Inappropriate extensions return through the unchanged platform error and `ERRORED` before `File.Exists`, process launch, or hook registration; an appropriate missing file reaches the unchanged missing-file boundary.
- **Preserved contracts and caveats:** `StartScript`, settings/schema, type ID/public ABI, logs/statuses/returns, `ProcessStartInfo`, arguments/output protocol, minimum/maximum/queue/timing/failure/idle/shutdown lifecycle, callers, and documentation remain unchanged. An accepted extension does not prove interpreter availability, permissions, executability, or process success; all non-Windows platforms share `.sh`; Windows runtime remains unvalidated because no Windows environment was available; wider platform/filesystem/runtime behavior remains unvalidated; and performance remains unmeasured.
- **Raw maintainer evidence (verbatim, 2026-07-27):** `Cases 1–18 passed on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, Bash [version]. Cases 19–20 were not run; no Windows environment was available.`
- **Disclosed normalization and result:** the literal placeholder `[version]` means no Bash version was supplied, so the recorded environment is `Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, using Bash (version not provided)`. The normalized result is 18 passed, 0 failed, and 2 unrun. Cases 1–18 are all 18/18 available non-Windows cases in the exact unchanged matrix and passed. Windows-only cases 19–20 were not run because no Windows environment was available; they remain unvalidated, are not failures, and establish no Windows behavior.
- **Static/runtime boundary:** agents performed no build, test, launch, script, service, API, browser, runtime, platform, filesystem, or performance exercise and issued no runtime result. The maintainer result is runtime evidence only for exact unchanged cases 1–18 on the recorded environment; it does not prove launchability, interpreter availability, permissions, executability, process success, wider platform/filesystem/runtime behavior, or performance.
- **Documentation and validation reviews:** Task 4 closure commit `08d58bb9145b692d506e964f6d3f516f6fe4f5fc` (`docs: record autoscaling launch-script validation`) was followed by focused documentation corrections `89df7a3ed1ec66fe03eaa7f568a755c71d03d4d8` (`docs: clarify autoscaling historical guard`) and `f9232169eca493ddafa553549d9c0ce4307a9eda` (`docs: clarify autoscaling base predicate`). Documentation re-reviews returned `DOCS_SPEC_APPROVED` and `DOCS_QUALITY_APPROVED` with no remaining documentation findings. The Task 5 validation-document specification review initially found that the audit completion range incorrectly said ranks `9 through 19`; focused correction commit `bc49bbad0fe4d2260807b4b8b86076431dc69233` (`docs: correct autoscaling validation range`) changed it to `9 through 20`, and successful same-reviewer re-review returned `VALIDATION_SPEC_APPROVED`.
