### Backend F14 — add/delete dirty publication can race the periodic persistence acknowledgement

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** Real configured-backend mutations publish monotonic generations only after visibility, non-real removal does not independently publish, and persistence acknowledges only a confirmed captured generation. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the normal build/launch workflow and the complete backend persistence matrix.
- **Boundary and exact evidence:** `BackendHandler.BackendsEdited` is a public unsynchronized `bool`; `AddNewOfType` sets it before constructing/adding the backend and `DeleteById` sets it before `TryRemove`. Edit and `BackendAPI.ToggleBackend` also set it, but after their principal mutations. Program's ten-second task checks the flag, assigns `false`, then calls `Save`; `SaveLock` serializes file writes but does not protect the dirty check/clear or catalog mutation. Shutdown checks the same flag before a final save.
- **Known maintained consumers and flow:** `BackendAPI.AddNewBackend` and `Installation.BackendComfy` call `AddNewOfType` for real, persisted backends. `BackendAPI.DeleteBackend` is the maintained configured-backend deletion route and passes its integer ID directly to `DeleteById`; a real ID is persistence-relevant (the method itself does not enforce `IsReal`). `AutoScalingBackend.StopOne` and two controlling-`SwarmSwarmBackend` removal/shutdown paths call the same method for known runtime-only non-real children: they share the early dirty publication even though `Save` skips those instances. Program is the sole maintained periodic `Save` caller, and shutdown is the other save path. If Program observes an early `true`, then clears and snapshots before a later real add/remove publishes to `AllBackends`, that operation has no remaining dirty notification. Edit/toggle set dirty after their principal mutations and do not independently establish this exact missed-snapshot ordering.
- **Problem/risk and certainty:** a real add/delete can acknowledge dirty state before the persisted catalog change exists, so a successfully returned runtime mutation can remain only in memory and be lost on restart. Non-real deletion cannot itself be omitted from a file that excludes it, but it still drives the same dirty/save mechanism and can trigger an unnecessary snapshot. Both distinctions and the real-mutation interleaving are **statically confirmed**; timing/frequency is runtime-dependent.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve `Backends.fds` structure, secret filtering, IDs/order semantics, ten-second coalescing, API response timing, final shutdown save, and non-real exclusion. `SaveLock` and process-wide atomic counters provide local synchronization patterns, but no implementation is authorized by this audit.
- **Bounded opportunity, migration, and rollback:** either synchronize real catalog mutation with snapshot acknowledgement, or introduce a monotonic generation protocol with all three required edges: publish/increment only after each persisted mutation is visible; let the saver capture a target generation before building/writing its snapshot; and advance the saved generation only after a successful write and only when a post-write comparison shows no newer generation, otherwise leave work pending. Do not mark non-real deletion dirty unless a separately serialized parent setting changed. Keep existing mutation/`Save` facades initially. Coordinate F21 so exceptions never acknowledge the target and the loop remains available to retry. Rollback must revert the generation/exception protocol together; retaining a compatibility `BackendsEdited` view must not restore pre-mutation publication or clear-before-save behavior.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** enumerate both real add callers, the real API delete, every non-real `DeleteById` caller, edit/toggle, both save paths, and the serialized-instance filter. Prove each real mutation publishes a later generation, mutation during snapshot forces another pass, only a successful unchanged-generation snapshot is acknowledged, failure remains pending, and non-real removal alone does not require persistence. Maintainer validation should overlap installation/API add, API delete, edit, and toggle with periodic save and shutdown; exercise autoscaler/remote-child deletion; inject one failed write; restart repeatedly; and verify the final acknowledged real catalog/settings survive without redundant saves for runtime-only children.

### Backend F15 — backend loading phase completion on every storage outcome

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.**
- **Historical boundary and exact evidence:** at design base `bcf96879d9d4769ce5913d8afd4f8a59d199d3eb`, static `BackendHandler.IsLoading` started `true`; `LoadInternal` started `InternalInitMonitor`, then returned early for missing/unreadable/null `Backends.fds`, while its sole completion assignment followed the non-null backend loop. `DoInitBackend` incremented `CountBackendsFastLoaded` and captured the startup-only delay after the first fast-capable load while the stale flag remained true. That trailing-assignment/early-return defect is historical evidence, not current source behavior.
- **Implemented boundary and outcomes:** plan `5e520b4a82a9d28cb468312445672bd05d7bc891` led to production commit/source head `f3313c7b56774833c7765e4770d90d5a2fff1158`. The integrated history contains the design, plan, and production commits; source-path filtering returns only the production commit. It changes exactly `BackendHandler.LoadInternal()` in `src/Backends/BackendHandler.cs`, one file with `38 insertions(+), 32 deletions(-)`. The log and successful monitor start remain before one outer `try`; the existing storage body is inside it; one `finally` contains the sole phase-completion assignment; and no outer catch was added.
- **Outcome and exception semantics:** missing file, missing directory, other handled read errors, null file, empty valid file, unknown-type continuation, and normal success now complete the storage phase. Unexpected post-monitor root-enumeration, entry-processing, or scheduling exceptions complete the phase and propagate unchanged; partial progress remains possible. Pre-`try` log or monitor-start failure does not clear the flag. Completion means storage enumeration/scheduling ended, not that backends are ready.
- **Captured-delay and compatibility boundaries:** configured entries still call unchanged `DoInitBackend` while the phase is true. Its synchronous counter increment, `bool shouldWait = count > 1 && IsLoading` capture, first-fast behavior, delay formula, and queued path are unchanged, and captured decisions survive later flag clearing. `Load()`, the counter, monitor, request loop, retry/status behavior, callers, persistence, ABI, parser policy, and all other source remain unchanged.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** independent static-only reviews returned `SOURCE_SPEC_APPROVED` and `SOURCE_QUALITY_APPROVED` with no findings. Exact fixed-range/source-scope, public/protected declaration, unchanged `Program.cs` and `BackendAPI.cs`, caller/consumer inventory, captured-delay, whitespace, clean-index, and protected-work-isolation checks passed. Agents performed no build, test, launch, runtime, platform, filesystem, or performance exercise. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 confirmed on 2026-07-27 that the exact unchanged 14-case matrix result was `All 14 passed on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.` This runtime result is limited to that recorded platform/filesystem; other platforms/filesystems remain unvalidated, and no performance claim is made. Failures before monitor startup still do not clear the flag; the duplicate `Load()` guard does not enter the phase; unexpected entry-processing exceptions still propagate and can leave partial progress and prevent the remainder of `Load()`; phase completion is not backend readiness; and no rollback or recovery behavior changed.
- **Rollback:** restore the former trailing completion assignment and remove the outer storage-phase `try/finally`; no data, configuration, format, API, or ABI migration is involved.

### Backend F16 — two maintained generation loops admitted one task above the user's simultaneous limit

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.** Approved design base `e48920bee4db248e0dfa91bbc1099a9161509f05`, plan `74b64f527ccebd01c8bd15a8835a35d72282b377`, and production source head `1fa1d5356c1446cd9d5ec9c33b76eddf1e06deb7` form the integrated design-to-source history; filtering to the production paths returns only the production commit.
- **Historical boundary and exact evidence:** before `1fa1d5356c1446cd9d5ec9c33b76eddf1e06deb7`, `T2IAPI.GenT2I_Internal` and `ImageBatchToolExtension.GenBatchRun_Internal` both removed completed tasks, waited only while `tasks.Count > max_degrees`, then added another task. With count equal to the limit, both added one more. `RoleData.MaxT2ISimultaneous` explicitly describes how many images a user may have actively generating and as a many-backend fairness protection; `User.CalcMaxT2ISimultaneous` supplies the effective bound.
- **Known maintained consumers and flow:** normal HTTP/WebSocket T2I uses the first loop; the built-in Image Batch streaming route uses the second. Each added task can independently acquire a backend and enter `GenerateLive`; backend `MaxUsages` limits each instance but does not correct either handler invocation's local off-by-one. No aggregate account-wide guarantee is inferred. Grid Generator has its own scheduler and is not evidence for this comparison defect.
- **Historical problem/risk and certainty:** the original predicates permitted `limit + 1` concurrent local orchestration/generation tasks, violating each handler invocation's intended request-local admission boundary. The setting was described as a many-backend fairness protection, but this historical evidence did not establish an aggregate account-wide fairness boundary. The historical arithmetic and reachable scheduling path are **statically confirmed**; whether all admitted tasks concurrently reached `GenerateLive` depended on backend capacity/timing.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve the effective-limit calculation, request order, cancellation, keep-alives, batch indices, task error logging, backend capacity, and extension route/result contracts.
- **Implemented boundary, migration, and rollback:** production changes exactly `src/WebAPI/T2IAPI.cs` and `src/BuiltinExtensions/ImageBatchTool/ImageBatchToolExtension.cs`, each `1 insertion, 1 deletion`; one added `=` changes each predicate to `while (tasks.Count >= max_degrees)`. After unchanged cleanup, each handler invocation admits only below its captured request-local effective limit. Rollback restores the two `>` predicates together.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** fixed-range review confirmed the exact two-file/two-character boundary, equality wait, and unchanged cleanup, wait, cancellation, ordering, keep-alive, index, error, output, webhook, claim, role, Grid Generator, backend, route, and ABI behavior; `SOURCE_SPEC_APPROVED` and `SOURCE_QUALITY_APPROVED` returned no findings. Agents performed no build, test, launch, runtime, platform, filesystem, or performance exercise. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 explicitly confirmed all 20 exact recorded cases passed on 2026-07-27 on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs. Cases 18 and 19 passed on their expected request-local basis: the aggregate count may exceed the role value. The implemented guarantee is request-local only; true account-wide enforcement and multi-user fairness validation remain out of scope, and no broader platform/filesystem or performance result is claimed.

### Backend F17 — pre-start model-load cancellation can strand pressure state and session claims

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Linux.** The final design was reviewed through `c67c0b8b`, the corrected plan and review criteria through `42467376`, and exact production range `42467376..25859d6e` contains one production commit changing only `src/Backends/BackendHandler.cs` (148 insertions, 40 deletions). Specification and quality reviews passed; agents performed no runtime validation.
- **Boundary and original evidence:** before `25859d6e`, `LoadHighestPressureNow` set `highestPressure.IsLoading = true`, created one `Session.GenClaim(modelLoads: 1)` per pressure session, then called `Task.Factory.StartNew(action, cancel)`. Reservation assignment and all cleanup—clearing `ReserveModelLoad`/`IsLoading`, disposing those claims, recording a failed backend, and reassigning loaded-model flags—were inside the delegate's `try/finally`. A canceled scheduling token could cancel the task before the delegate started, so none of that cleanup executed.
- **Known maintained consumers and flow:** `T2IEngine.CreateImageTask` is the sole maintained `GetNextT2IBackend` caller and passes its session claim's interrupt token. Session interruption can therefore race between creation of the model-load claims and delegate start. The waiting request eventually observes cancellation and `Complete` releases its request pressure count, but that method does not own the separately created model-load claims or reset `IsLoading`.
- **Original problem/risk and certainty:** cancellation before delegate execution could leave claims in each active session's `Claims` and leave `LoadingModels` counters elevated. If other requests kept the same pressure registered, its `IsLoading` also remained true and prevented a replacement load; a sole canceled request removed the pressure through its separate request-completion path but still did not dispose the model-load claims. Status/cancellation bookkeeping could therefore remain wrong after the request ended. The original cleanup placement and task-cancellation semantics were **statically confirmed**; hitting the scheduling window was runtime-dependent.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve pressure heuristic/order, request token semantics, loader selection/reservation, session status frames, failure reasons, model-load cancellation, and one cleanup execution. `GenClaim` is explicitly disposable and other generation paths use lexical `using`/`finally` ownership.
- **Implemented boundary, migration, and rollback:** one interlocked lifecycle now gives pre-start cancellation, setup/attachment failure, or the started delegate exclusive cleanup ownership. Pre-start cleanup touches only published selected-pressure state and captured claims; started cleanup retains reservation, mismatch, selected-state, claim, and refresh ordering. Cleanup diagnostics are nonthrowing, and each primitive is isolated behind a nonthrowing boundary; the winning owner makes one `Dispose` attempt per captured claim, with one separately guarded `GC.SuppressFinalize` attempt after failure. `Session.cs`, scheduling policy, token domains, public signatures, and scheduling-caller refusal/failure delivery are unchanged. The adjacent `LoadModelOnAll` shutdown/reservation behavior, the underlying asynchronous `LoadModel` continuation/cancellation contract, stale `ModelRequestPressure.Requests`/`Sessions`, and scheduler measurement/optimization under P5 remain unchanged and out of scope. Rollback is confined to this one ownership change.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** static review confirmed the exact source range/scope, lifecycle transitions, cancellation continuation, post-failure suppression, ordered cleanup, unchanged `Session.cs`, and caller-selected-pressure coupling. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the complete Linux matrix passed on 2026-07-25: publication/setup and attachment/delegate races; sole/shared and matching/different-owner pre-start cancellation; busy, `(none)`, load, and shutdown started paths; success, readable failure, mismatch, callback/refusal/failure delivery, competing pressure, and repeated dispatch races; every named pre-start/started cleanup primitive failure; partial disposal with successful suppression; suppression failure; and independent diagnostic construction, `ReadableString()`, and `Logs.Error` failures. Original exceptions, nonfaulting cancellation cleanup, later cleanup, selected/caller ownership, one winning-owner attempt, baseline when primitives succeeded, and later progress all passed. Constructor failure before a claim returns, partial cleanup-primitive/disposal/resource failure, and finalizer-suppression failure retain the bounded caveats recorded in the design. Windows and other-platform runtime behavior and performance remain unvalidated; agents performed no runtime/build/test.

### Backend F18 — backend shutdown tracks work in a concurrently mutated List

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.** Approved design base `f17e09557157f9c3ed13841c368885711636d96f` is followed in the integrated design-to-source history by documentation-plan commit `880d03df131977126c2557dbc5844568289e06be` and production commit `efe5e32ad9ed93ae1727f613e19c91f27bbbfe03`; source-path filtering returns only `efe5e32a`. Production changes exactly `BackendHandler.Shutdown()` in `src/Backends/BackendHandler.cs`, with `24 insertions(+), 14 deletions(-)`.
- **Original boundary and exact evidence:** `BackendHandler.Shutdown` created `List<(BackendData, Task)> tasks`, added one `Task.Run` wrapper per backend, and each wrapper later called `tasks.Add` with `DoShutdownNow`. Concurrently, the shutdown thread repeatedly enumerated and replaced `tasks` with incomplete entries. `List<T>` does not provide synchronization for those concurrent adds/enumerations, and no handler-local lock protected the collection.
- **Known maintained consumers and current flow:** Core `Program.Shutdown` calls this owner after global cancellation. The implementation captures one `AllBackends.Values` entry snapshot, controller-publishes one stable task per snapshot backend, keeps the usage grace and direct awaited `DoShutdownNow()` in that task, and controller-derives pending views until every stable task completes. Backend-specific grace or shutdown faults are logged with backend identity and readable detail and isolated, so other backend tasks, the done-generating webhook, final persistence, and later shutdown owners remain reachable.
- **Original problem/risk and certainty:** concurrent mutation could throw during enumeration, lose a newly appended shutdown task when the local list was replaced, or allow the loop to exit before all backend shutdown work finished. The former unsynchronized access and possible interleavings are **statically confirmed**; observed failure frequency was runtime-dependent.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve parallel backend shutdown, the five-second usage grace, per-backend `DoShutdownNow`, progress logging, webhook ordering, final save, and Program's wider shutdown order. Core F10's atomic outer gate was complementary but did not make the former inner list safe; the two ownership boundaries remain separate.
- **Implemented migration and rollback:** one stable handler-owned task per snapshot backend now includes both grace wait and awaited shutdown, and only the controller mutates its pending view. The guard, signals, grace rules/message, progress behavior, webhook, persistence, callers, backend implementations, and public ABI are unchanged. Rollback is the handler-local task aggregation.
- **Static verification, caveats, and maintainer validation:** fixed-range source/whitespace review and independent reviews returned `SOURCE_SPEC_APPROVED` and `SOURCE_QUALITY_APPROVED` with no findings. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 explicitly confirmed all 14 exact matrix cases passed on 2026-07-27 on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs, covering empty/immediate, grace/forced, mixed parallel/slow pending, single/multiple fault, real/non-real/autoscaled, progress, final-save, repeat-call, outer-gate overlap, and repeated stress outcomes. Late additions remain outside the captured snapshot; a non-completing backend still waits indefinitely; and autoscaling parent/controlled-child internals remain unchanged, so one handler task per snapshot entry is not an exactly-once claim across internal owners. Agents performed no build, test, launch, runtime, platform, filesystem, or performance exercise. Windows, other Linux distributions/filesystems, and performance remain unvalidated or unmeasured.

### Backend F19 — output-save failure can leave a permanent pending-byte entry

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.**
- **Implemented boundary and static evidence:** the stable integrated range base is `e5fcebf781ad68822d6269f73b7d1b182e54997b`; `216949edac9e80db91cb0b639b66c88d2d642b83` is the pre-closure static implementation head, before this documentation-only closure commit. The complete interleaved range contains `60520338`, `24a7df6b`, `d05b8757`, `aa2c9fbd`, `ac2b8bd6`, `6d230e4c`, `632ae1cc`, `f401d4e2`, `87c576f9`, `d15c364e`, `35e939b0`, and `216949ed`; path filtering to production excludes the documentation commits and yields `24a7df6b`, `d05b8757`, `aa2c9fbd`, `ac2b8bd6`, `6d230e4c`, and `216949ed`. Production changes exactly `src/Accounts/Session.cs` (`289` insertions, `18` deletions), `src/WebAPI/BackendAPI.cs` (`1` insertion, `1` deletion), and `src/WebAPI/ImageHistoryAPI.cs` (`30` insertions, `10` deletions): `320 insertions(+), 29 deletions(-)` overall. Final correction `216949ed` changes only `Session.cs` and `ImageHistoryAPI.cs`; `BackendAPI.cs` already called `Session.ClearOutputFilenameReservations`.
- **Implemented boundary, compatibility, and caveats:** pending cleanup now removes only the exact path-and-task pair. Failure cleanup does not add the successful read-through delay, while full success retains the existing ten-second window. The public dictionary declarations/types/static identity and both maintained readers remain unchanged; `SaveImage`'s signature, URLs, templates, work order, no-save behavior, and reporting contract remain unchanged. Independent static reviews returned `SPEC_APPROVED` and `QUALITY_APPROVED` with no issues. The direct-extension same-key race remains outside the coordinator guarantee; partial-output/failure artifacts remain possible, and failed-deletion protection remains until clear/restart. Agents made no build, test, runtime, platform, or performance claim.
- **Original-baseline boundary and exact evidence:** at the pinned original audit baseline, after `Session.SaveImage`'s early `SaveFiles = false` data-URL return, its persisted branch inserted `StillSavingFiles[fullPath]`, started a fire-and-forget `Utilities.RunCheckedTask`, and removed the entry only after conversion await, media/sidecar writes, preview/index updates, and a ten-second delay. There was no `finally`. Any exception before removal was caught only by `RunCheckedTask`, which logged and completed normally. `WebServer` output reads and `T2IParamTypes.FilePathToDataString` preferred and awaited the stored `Task<byte[]>` whenever the key remained.
- **Original-baseline maintained consumers and flow:** normal T2I and Grid Generator could bypass `Session.SaveImage` for explicit no-save output; normal T2I, Image History add, and Grid called it for their remaining outputs, but only user-enabled persisted saves entered this pending-byte path. Its persisted URL was returned/streamed immediately. Conversion could itself return `null` after logging, and write/preview/index operations could fail. A stranded successful pending-byte task kept serving memory indefinitely; a stranded faulted/null-derived task made later readers repeatedly fail even if external recovery changed the file.
- **Original-baseline problem/risk and certainty:** failure bypassed transient-cache cleanup and could retain full output bytes/tasks for process lifetime while overriding normal disk reads for that path. The missing cleanup edge was **statically confirmed** at the pinned baseline; failure occurrence and retained byte volume were runtime-dependent.
- **Original-baseline compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** the migration needed to preserve immediate URL publication, just-saved read-through behavior, the ten-second successful retention window, sidecar/preview/index order, error logging, and all path keys/readers. It did not require changing frontend history behavior.
- **Implemented migration and rollback:** exact path-and-task removal in `finally` is now implemented, retaining the ten-second delay after success while removing immediately after failure. Whether failure needs a new observable API contract remains separately scoped rather than coupled to this migration. Restoring the original pending-cleanup placement is permitted only as part of the coordinated Rank 12 rollback of pending-task cleanup, reservation ownership, deletion acquisition, and coordinated clear; it must not be reverted independently.
- **Historical static verification and current maintainer validation:** the original requirement was to trace every post-insert throw/return and both readers, proving successful retention and failed cleanup; the implemented source passed that static review. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 explicitly confirmed all 37 exact Rank 12 cases passed on Garuda Linux (Arch-based) using Btrfs on 2026-07-27, including conversion-null, media/sidecar write failure, preview/index failure, immediate and file-backed reads, and return of pending/expiring counts to baseline. Windows and other Linux/filesystem combinations remain runtime-unvalidated; no performance measurement is claimed.

### Backend F20 — output filename reservations grow for process lifetime

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated on Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs.**
- **Implemented boundary and static evidence:** the stable integrated range base is `e5fcebf781ad68822d6269f73b7d1b182e54997b`; `216949edac9e80db91cb0b639b66c88d2d642b83` is the pre-closure static implementation head, before this documentation-only closure commit. The complete interleaved range contains `60520338`, `24a7df6b`, `d05b8757`, `aa2c9fbd`, `ac2b8bd6`, `6d230e4c`, `632ae1cc`, `f401d4e2`, `87c576f9`, `d15c364e`, `35e939b0`, and `216949ed`; the production-only path history is `24a7df6b`, `d05b8757`, `aa2c9fbd`, `ac2b8bd6`, `6d230e4c`, and `216949ed`. Its exact projection is `src/Accounts/Session.cs` (`289` insertions, `18` deletions), `src/WebAPI/BackendAPI.cs` (`1` insertion, `1` deletion), and `src/WebAPI/ImageHistoryAPI.cs` (`30` insertions, `10` deletions), totaling `320 insertions(+), 29 deletions(-)`. Final correction `216949ed` changes only `Session.cs` and `ImageHistoryAPI.cs`; no Task 4A textual change was needed in `BackendAPI.cs` because it already called `Session.ClearOutputFilenameReservations`.
- **Implemented boundary, compatibility, and caveats:** a nested path-to-generation-to-owner coordinator gives exact-owner cleanup and retains the aggregate public key through the final owner. Active maintained owners gate deletion before mutation with a retryable JObject; inactive owners can coexist with an active deletion retry. Inactive transition is synchronous; failed saves and successful deletions expire after ten seconds, while partial or failed deletions remain until system-RAM clear or restart. Clear removes inactive/legacy entries while continuously preserving active private/public state. Public field declarations/types/static identity, collision templates, disk checks, URLs, save order, and no-save paths remain unchanged. Independent static reviews returned `SPEC_APPROVED` and `QUALITY_APPROVED` with no issues. Direct-extension same-key mutation remains a caveat; partial-output and deletion artifacts remain possible; administrative clear deliberately removes inactive failed-deletion protection without repairing orphan state. Agents made no build, test, runtime, platform, or performance claim.
- **Original-baseline boundary and exact evidence:** at the pinned original audit baseline, `Session.RecentlyBlockedFilenames` was a static concurrent dictionary. Every `SaveImage` invocation that passed the user `SaveFiles` check and selected a persisted full path added that path; `ImageHistoryAPI.DeleteImage` independently added the deleted standardized path. No time-based or per-save removal existed; the sole maintained clear was `BackendAPI.FreeBackendMemory(system_ram: true)`. Each later persisted save folded all keys whose strings started with the target folder into its collision set while holding `User.UserLock`.
- **Original-baseline maintained consumers and flow:** persisted normal T2I, history-add, and Grid outputs added entries through `SaveImage`; their data-URL bypasses did not. History deletion added entries directly; future persisted saves used the set to avoid reusing those paths alongside current directory contents. The map crossed users and output roots for the whole process.
- **Original-baseline problem/risk and certainty:** one key was retained per distinct saved/deleted output until an unrelated admin RAM-clear operation or process restart, so memory and the per-save key scan grew with lifetime output activity; deleted names also remained unavailable for reuse despite the field comment saying “recently.” Growth and permanent reservation were **statically confirmed** at the pinned baseline; material memory/latency and desired name-reuse policy remained runtime/maintainer judgments.
- **Original-baseline compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** the migration needed to preserve collision safety for concurrent background saves, deletion races, `[number]`/suffix behavior, user locks, existing on-disk files, and per-user output roots. It could not replace the reservation owner with the history index, whose completion/freshness contract differed.
- **Implemented migration and rollback:** the owner-aware lifetime migration is now implemented through exact-generation ownership, active/inactive transitions, bounded expiry, retained failed-deletion state, and coordinated clear while retaining the global public facade. Rollback would restore non-expiring entries and must revert the coordinated ownership unit together.
- **Historical static verification and current maintainer validation:** the original requirement was to enumerate all add/read/clear paths, prove active saves/deletes could not reuse a name, and verify bounded cleanup; the implemented source passed that static review. Separately, maintainer Reaper176 explicitly confirmed all 37 exact Rank 12 cases passed on Garuda Linux (Arch-based) using Btrfs on 2026-07-27, including rapid/concurrent save and delete races, pre-mutation refusal, inactive-owner retry coexistence, final-owner release, multi-user/folder isolation, `system_ram` true/false behavior with active preservation, extension compatibility, baseline counts, and retained failed-deletion counts until clear/restart. Windows and other Linux/filesystem combinations remain runtime-unvalidated, and key-count/collision-selection performance remains unmeasured.

### Backend F21 — a backend-save exception terminates periodic persistence and can abort outer shutdown

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** Failed periodic writes retain pending work and retry, verified authoritative commits are distinguished from true failure, and one failed final attempt cannot abort later shutdown cleanup or change the requested exit code. Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the normal build/launch workflow and the complete backend persistence matrix.
- **Boundary and exact evidence:** `BackendHandler.Save` holds `SaveLock`, builds FDS, and calls `FDSUtility.SaveToFile` without an exception boundary. Program's ten-second persistence task is a raw `Task.Run` loop and calls `Backends.Save` without `try/catch` or `RunCheckedTask`; its task reference is not retained, so one save exception exits the only periodic writer. `BackendHandler.Shutdown` also calls `Save` without a boundary when dirty, and `Program.Shutdown` calls `Backends.Shutdown` before sessions, proxy, model handlers, extensions, output metadata, temp cleanup, and log flush without per-owner isolation.
- **Known maintained consumers and flow:** add/edit/delete/toggle operations depend on the periodic writer and final dirty save. Filesystem/path/serialization failures can originate at the FDS write. Server-settings persistence is a distinct owner that catches/logs its write failure (Core F6); it does not protect backend persistence.
- **Problem/risk and certainty:** a transient backend save failure silently ends future periodic persistence attempts, and a dirty shutdown save failure can unwind the outer shutdown sequence before later owners are disposed/flushed. Both exception propagation paths are **statically confirmed**; actual storage failure and framework handling of the unobserved task/shutdown callback are runtime-dependent.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve FDS schema/snapshot, dirty/coalescing semantics, save logging, lock serialization, shutdown ordering, and visibility of persistent failures. Do not suppress errors without retaining dirty state and a retry path. `Utilities.RunCheckedTask` and settings-save logging are local error-reporting patterns, but backend persistence additionally needs retry acknowledgement compatible with F14.
- **Bounded opportunity, migration, and rollback:** introduce F14's post-mutation generation publication and successful-snapshot acknowledgement first (or in the same focused change); a periodic write exception must log, leave the target generation unsaved, and keep the loop alive for a later retry. Then isolate final-save failure so shutdown logs it and continues later cleanup without falsely advancing the saved generation. Keep storage format unchanged. Rollback must treat F14's publication/acknowledgement and these failure boundaries as one protocol: do not restore clear-before-save or allow a caught failure to mark state clean.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** trace the sole periodic task, all save calls, generation publication/acknowledgement, and Program's remaining shutdown owners; prove only a successful unchanged-generation snapshot advances saved state, failure retains a pending save and live retry loop, and final failure cannot skip later shutdown cleanup. Validate a temporarily unwritable backend file/directory, recovery and retry, mutation during snapshot, repeated edits, and shutdown under write failure, then confirm sessions/models/extensions/metadata/logs still close and the final recoverable catalog persists.

### Comfy F22 — backend-local capability discovery is published as one mutable global feature set

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented; awaiting maintainer validation.** The inclusive production range is `8bcc4677` through `670ddbf9` (`8bcc4677^..670ddbf9` in Git notation), comprising 13 production commits across nine source files. Design commit `efa1e48b` is the fixed pre-plan boundary and plan commit `3978b00d` is documentation only.
- **Boundary and exact historical evidence:** At the pinned audit base, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIAPIAbstractBackend.cs::LoadValueSet` assigned instance-local `RawObjectInfo`, `NodeTypes`, `Models`, and `ModelFolderFormat`, then called `ComfyUIBackendExtension.AssignValuesFromRaw`. In `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIBackendExtension.cs`, that method locked `ValueAssignmentLocker` but added/removed detected capabilities in static `FeaturesSupported`/`FeaturesDiscardIfNotFound`; the latter permanently dropped a feature after any backend confirmed it. `ComfyUIAPIAbstractBackend.SupportedFeatures` returned the same global `FeaturesSupported` enumerable for every instance, with only the folder separator appended.
- **Known maintained consumers:** `T2IEngine.BackendMatcherFor` compares each backend's `SupportedFeatures` with request flags; `GenerateLive`, `LoadModel`, `ComfyGetGeneratedWorkflow`, and direct `ComfyUser` fixup select workflow behavior from it; `BackendHandler.GetAllSupportedFeatures`, `BasicAPIFeatures`, `AdminAPI`, and `BackendAPI` expose aggregate/per-backend features. Local API/self-start backends and linked `SwarmSwarmBackend` object-info both feed the global interpreter.
- **Current data/control flow:** local refreshes and linked object-info refreshes use per-owner gates, build complete candidates before the global lock, and publish frozen per-owner node/feature snapshots. Local scheduling, generation, model loading, workflow preview/construction, direct prompt routing, folder fixup, and raw validation consume the selected backend's snapshot. Linked scheduling consumes an immutable remote-status feature snapshot while linked raw validation consumes its separately frozen object-info node set. `ComfyCapabilityRegistry` reinterprets stored owner evidence after direct public compatibility mutations and republishes the same `FeaturesSupported` object plus an immutable aggregate copy. Deletion removes only the owner that has already left `AllBackends`; edit, reload, idle, and shutdown preparation retain last-good evidence.
- **Historical problem/risk and certainty:** heterogeneous Comfy instances could all advertise a node/feature found on only one instance, allowing scheduler acceptance and graph construction for an instance that lacked the required node. Concurrent refresh could also mutate the non-concurrent set while a reader enumerated it. The shared declarations, mutation, deferred enumeration, and per-backend consumers were **statically confirmed** and motivated rank 8; runtime frequency was not asserted.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve the public mutable `FeaturesSupported`, `FeaturesDiscardIfNotFound`, and `NodeToFeatureMap` surfaces for extensions; global parameter visibility remains an aggregate concern, while backend matching/generation requires an instance snapshot. Preserve feature IDs, presumptive local-install hints, object-info authority, parser callbacks/order, remote validation, and folder-separator flags.
- **Implemented migration and rollback:** `ComfyCapabilityCatalog` is a pure interpreter; `ComfyCapabilityRegistry` owns frozen per-owner evidence, compatibility overrides, and aggregate publication; local and linked readers consume the matching immutable authority; and the public collections retain their field types and identities. Rollback must restore local/remote readers, registry aggregation, compatibility reconciliation, owner removal, and candidate-first publication together; delegating only some readers back to the aggregate would reintroduce split authority.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** final static review found 54 primary compatibility-search lines/55 occurrences plus seven maintained supplemental node-map writers, 14 `SupportedFeatures`/aggregate lines, and 26 node/remote lines/29 occurrences. The four Task 8 negative searches found no maintained backend-local global-set decision, no remote compatibility-dictionary reader, no mutable linked-node cast, and only the three established public-field declaration assignments. The 52 direct gate/lock/I/O matches show that maintained core performs no direct network, WebSocket, process, shutdown, model, or GPU work under `ValueAssignmentLocker`; public parser dispatch remains under the lock, and arbitrary extension callback internals are outside this maintained-source static guarantee. `git diff --check efa1e48b..670ddbf9` is clean. The four protected maintainer files and `Data.pre-restore-2026-07-19/` were excluded. No agent ran a build, test, launcher, backend, browser, Comfy process, live concurrency exercise, or benchmark. Maintainer validation remains the heterogeneous local/remote, refresh/reload/removal, generated-workflow, exact/normal scheduling, raw validation, parameter visibility, failure, compatibility-mutation, parser-isolation, and concurrent status/list matrix.

### Comfy F23 — custom-workflow durability and publication ordering

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** `ComfyWorkflowStore` is the production transaction and maintained-access owner. Store delegation, reader coordination, journal/recovery, the fail-closed recovery gate, same-instance `CustomWorkflows` publication, stage/hash-verified cleanup, and content-redacted persistence diagnostics form one rollback unit. Comfy P8 remains a separate measurement prerequisite, and no performance claim is made.
- **Boundary and exact historical evidence:** At the pinned audit baseline, `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIWebAPI.cs::ComfySaveWorkflow` cleaned names and transformed the image, then called `ComfyDeleteWorkflow` when `replace` was present. It next assigned the new complete record into `ComfyUIBackendExtension.CustomWorkflows`, only afterward parsed `workflow`, `prompt`, `custom_params`, and `param_values` into the on-disk `JObject`, and finally called `File.WriteAllBytes`. `ComfyDeleteWorkflow` removed the cache entry and deleted/marked the file immediately; the manifest/cache readers were in `ComfyUIBackendExtension.cs::LoadWorkflowFiles`/`GetWorkflowByName`.
- **Known maintained consumers:** permission-gated save/read/list/delete routes; `CustomWorkflowParam` value/clean delegates; `GetRawWorkflowFrom` and standard generation; dynamic workflow parameter handling; startup/model refresh through `LoadWorkflowFiles`; and built-in example deletion markers. External users of the public `CustomWorkflows` field are a compatibility constraint.
- **Current data/control flow:** Candidate JSON/image validation precedes transaction creation. The store holds one lock across same-filesystem staging, durable prepared/committed journal phases, recoverable original moves, marker updates, and post-commit cache publication. Maintained readers share the lock and `RecoveryRequired` gate; recovery validates path/role/content ownership before mutation, cleanup hash-verifies owned stages, and refresh privately stages inventory before repopulating the same public dictionary instance. Persistence, rollback, and recovery failures retain content-redacted diagnostics.
- **Historical problem/risk and certainty:** At the pinned baseline, a failed save could return an error while the new workflow remained usable only in memory, and a failed replacement could additionally destroy the old durable workflow before the new one was valid/durable. That ordering and its partial states were **statically confirmed** and motivated rank 6; the current disposition is the implemented and maintainer-validated store protocol above.
- **Compatibility constraints and existing pattern:** preserve route names/arguments/permissions/errors, strict filename behavior, JSON field names and formatting, image metadata conversion, example `.deleted` markers, rename semantics, the public dictionary identity, and immediate visibility after a successful response. Do not silently accept invalid workflow JSON.
- **Implemented migration and rollback:** The focused store now fully validates/serializes before mutation, installs same-directory transaction stages and a durable journal, retains predecessor/destination recovery until commit, publishes only after durable success, and gates maintained access during unresolved recovery. Rollback must revert store delegation, read coordination, journal/recovery, gate, and publication together; the workflow schema requires no migration.
- **Static verification and maintainer validation:** Static review covered every maintained file/cache mutation and reader, recovery ordering and ownership checks, marker state, dictionary identity, redaction, and unchanged hydration. On 2026-07-24, maintainer Reaper176 confirmed normal build/launch/browse/generation plus new/overwrite/same-name/rename/collision/missing-predecessor/delete/example-marker behavior; each invalid JSON field and invalid image; image inherit/replace/clear/fallback; refresh/restart; concurrent readers; the available storage-failure cases; and interrupted-journal recovery on Linux. Windows received static cross-platform review only; no Windows runtime validation or performance claim is made.

## Compatibility and Reliability Risk Register

The register is ordered by potential user/data/process impact, then by breadth of the affected flow; ordering does not assert observed incident frequency.

| Risk | Trigger | Impact | Current mitigation | Static evidence | Runtime uncertainty | Action or measurement | Dependency |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| API error diagnostics disclose inputs | A server returns `data.error` for a request containing a key, session ID, image, metadata, or other private/large data. | Before mitigation, the browser console received the complete client-added request object in addition to the endpoint-generated error. | Commit `0d7076ce` removes client request serialization while preserving `data.error`, callbacks, and UI paths. | The prior shared `JSON.stringify(in_data)` plus concrete `SetAPIKey` caller and its one-line removal are confirmed. | Maintainer validation of console/UI/callback behavior and endpoint error content is pending. | Validate sensitive requests and the intentionally echoed invalid Image History sort; retain verbatim endpoint errors without client-added payloads. | Rank 1 implemented, awaiting maintainer validation; endpoint-generated error auditing/redaction remains separate. |
| Workflow diagnostics disclose secrets | Verbose/debug logging is enabled; preview/direct-proxy prompting, malformed submitted/private JSON, browser Comfy WebSocket parsing, or Comfy prompt/generation/ControlNet validation reaches a diagnostic or downstream error sink. | Credentials, private inputs, base64 prefixes, semantic identifiers, raw WebSocket messages, or submitted parser/conversion fragments can persist in server logs/error surfaces. | Seven formatter calls across ten protected lines emit only opaque aliases/counts/fixed markers; diagnostic parsing is date-neutral and validates through EOF; parameters are count-only; tag/private failures are fixed; `ComfySubmittedJson` replaces content-bearing parser failures with a fresh fixed-message `JsonReaderException` with no inner exception; the raw `ComfyUser` WebSocket log and dormant unsafe metadata-log example are removed. | Exactly nine original statements remain historically confirmed, plus 12 maintained parsing/unescaping invocations across `AwaitJobLive`, `TryIsValid`, tag unescape, metadata, stored container, four save fields, prompt, interrupt, and browser WebSocket paths. | Maintainer validation of all malformed and valid paths, log levels, opaque topology, and retained context is pending. | Implementation is complete pending maintainer sentinel validation across all expanded sources, including raw/stored malformed `TryIsValid` input and valid-JSON metadata/`enable_in_simple` conversion failures; retain the backend-response parser/output exclusion by provenance. | Rank 2; preserve opaque node/input ordering and aliased source edges, direct-proxy routing, ControlNet error, valid data/control flow, and extension compatibility independently of F4. |
| Generic API/T2I submitted-input diagnostics disclose content | Malformed initial API JSON, a malformed follow-up generation frame, a Grid dynamic-axis media object, or the former complete typed-input Verbose dump reaches a generic diagnostic. | Submitted JSON fragments, parser details, complete typed-input values, or media/base64 prefixes can reach protected logs/error surfaces. | `SubmittedInputJson` returns valid `JObject.Parse` results and replaces submitted parser failures with a fresh fixed inner-free `JsonReaderException`; `T2IParamSet` replaces valid-JSON media interpretation failures with a fresh fixed generic exception; the protected successful-generation Verbose diagnostic is count-only while the adjacent Info-level selected-model line is preserved. | `S1` confirms five source boundaries; initial HTTP/WebSocket and follow-up frame parsing use the submitted boundary, one shared dynamic-media parser serves image/audio/video conversion, and Grid axis application directly reaches it while ordinary T2I validation rejects the malformed JSON-looking value first. | Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the named build/runtime/sentinel matrix; no benchmark or performance measurement is claimed. | Retain the validated initial HTTP/WebSocket and follow-up parsing, fixed post-parse media failures, Grid `Task.Run` → `ExToError`/`ReadableString()` → WebSocket error-response path, count-only Verbose diagnostic, and preserved selected-model Info line. | `S1` is implemented and maintainer-validated; rank 3 is unblocked, while `S1` remains outside the unchanged 32 numbered ranks and preserves general JSON, backend-response/persisted-data, and Comfy exclusions. |
| OAuth auth cookie lacked request-aware Secure | A known Google OAuth user logs in through an HTTP, direct HTTPS, or TLS-terminating proxy path. | Before implementation, the browser could attach a long-lived login credential to a later insecure request. | **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** The OAuth writer sets `Secure = HttpContext.Request.IsHttps`, exactly matching password login/logout; cookie name/value, HttpOnly, one-year expiry, SameSite, session creation/storage/validation, redirect, OAuth registration, readers, and protected consumers are unchanged. | Writer alignment is static; no forwarded-header, trusted-proxy, Kestrel, settings, launcher, or Web-pipeline behavior changed. | Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the named HTTP, direct HTTPS, supported-proxy, OAuth/password login/logout, registration/redirect, and protected-consumer matrix; no benchmark or performance measurement is claimed. | Retain the validated behavior; trusted proxy scheme establishment remains separately scoped, and raw forwarding headers remain untrusted and out of scope. | Preserve all cookie/token/session contracts and supported proxy deployments; do not conflate transport-flag alignment with trusted scheme establishment. |
| Settings response diverges from memory/disk | A mixed settings request has invalid paths, or persistence fails. | Rejected non-path values can remain committed; successful response can describe non-durable state. | Paths alone are snapshotted/restored; save failures are logged. | Mutation/save/validation ordering, partial restore, and void caught save are confirmed. | Path/write failure frequency is unknown. | Make persistence observable and validate before publication or roll back the full submitted set. | Preserve settings format, reflection metadata, secret placeholders, and runtime side effects. |
| Backend add/delete is not durably acknowledged | A real installation/API add or API delete publishes dirty before catalog mutation while the periodic loop clears before snapshot/save. | A successfully returned persisted-catalog change can be lost on restart; non-real child deletion also causes unnecessary dirty saves. | `SaveLock` serializes writes and shutdown may save when a later notification remains. | Both real add callers, real API delete, non-real delete callers, early setters, clear-before-save loop, and real-only serialization are confirmed. | Race frequency depends on mutation/save timing. | Publish a generation after real mutation; capture before snapshot; acknowledge only a successful write with no newer generation, or synchronize mutation/snapshot acknowledgement. | Coordinate failure/retry/rollback with F21; preserve FDS schema, coalescing, secrets, non-real exclusion, IDs, and API timing. |
| Backend save failure stops persistence/cleanup | FDS save throws in the periodic loop or dirty backend shutdown. | Future periodic saves stop; shutdown can skip sessions/models/extensions/metadata/log cleanup. | Save serialization uses a lock; outer process events may invoke shutdown again. | Uncaught save call paths and subsequent shutdown order are confirmed. | Storage failure and framework task handling vary. | Retain dirty state/retry periodically, and log/isolate final-save failure so shutdown continues. | Coordinate dirty acknowledgement with F14; preserve FDS format, ordering, and visible errors. |
| Workflow save/replace partially commits | Replacement JSON parsing or filesystem write fails after predecessor deletion/cache publication in the historical flow. | Before implementation, the old durable workflow could be deleted, or the failed new value could remain memory-only and visible. | **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** `ComfyWorkflowStore` validates first, commits through a durable journal/recovery protocol, gates coordinated readers, preserves dictionary identity, and publishes after commit. | Store ownership, lock/gate coverage, journal ordering, stage/hash cleanup, same-instance refresh, and redacted diagnostics are statically confirmed. | Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the agreed Linux runtime transaction/recovery matrix on 2026-07-24; Windows received static review only. | Retain the complete store/read/journal/recovery/gate/publication rollback unit; P8 remains measurement-only. | Preserve JSON format, routes, public map identity, example markers, stable local-filesystem contract, and cross-platform behavior; no performance claim. |
| Download replacement/cancellation contract was incomplete | A direct-call destination was longer than the response, or the model-UI caller canceled before response headers. | Before implementation, stale trailing bytes could survive verified chunks and caller cancellation could wait on the request send. | **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** Linked-token send/stream acquisition precedes `OK`-accepted truncating create and worker startup. Known-length mismatch, hash mismatch, and writer failure retain deletion; unknown-length producer failure or cancellation may leave a partial target. | The exact range `24157f35^..051a5bb5` changes only `src/Utils/Utilities.cs`; ordering, token propagation, and the seven-direct/three-indirect caller inventory are statically confirmed. | Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed all 14 approved cases passed on Linux on 2026-07-25. Windows runtime and performance remain unvalidated; no agent runtime claim is made. | Retain the validated behavior, recorded unknown-length legacy caveats, and caller ownership; the caveats remain uncorrected and are not claimed fixed. | Rank 9 implemented and maintainer-validated; preserve retries, progress, hashes, fixed installer paths, model UI target/success ownership, workflow-generator catch cleanup, installation common-model behavior, and both Comfy model-support download/refresh paths. |
| Model catalog is partially published | Historically, runtime model paths changed while model/parameter/generation readers ran. | Before mitigation, readers could see empty/partial categories or shut-down handlers. | **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** `Program` now owns one write-bound path transition, publishes a complete seven-key catalog into the same dictionary, and coordinates maintained readers; download/refresh work uses explicit phases. | The original outer-dictionary clear/repopulate and missing writer claim remain historical evidence; the final static inventory contains 71 matching lines across 20 maintained consumers with two startup-only, two write-side, 57 lexically claimed, and ten centralized-provider lines. | Maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the recorded eight-case matrix on Linux on 2026-07-24; Windows runtime behavior and performance remain unvalidated. | Retain the coordinated owner/read boundary and confirmed compatibility behavior; do not reopen snapshot or performance work without new evidence. | Preserve dictionary identity, all seven category keys/path expressions, public facade/ABI, events, settings semantics, destinations, metadata, handler behavior, payloads, and extension coordination. |
| Comfy capabilities cross backend boundaries | Comfy instances expose different object-info, or refresh overlaps feature/status reads. | Before implementation, an incompatible backend could be selected/build a missing node; mutable-set enumeration could fault. | **Implemented; awaiting maintainer validation.** Frozen local and linked snapshots now separate backend decisions from the same-object legacy aggregate; per-owner gates and deletion hooks retain last-good/lifecycle ownership. | The inclusive production range is `8bcc4677` through `670ddbf9`; final inventories and negative searches find no maintained backend-local global-set read, remote compatibility-dictionary read, or mutable linked-node cast. | Heterogeneous, failure, lifecycle, concurrency, extension-compatibility, and cross-platform behavior remain pending maintainer validation. | Run the recorded local/remote heterogeneous capability, refresh/failure, lifecycle, concurrency, parser, and direct compatibility-mutation matrix. | Rank 8 implemented awaiting maintainer validation; preserve public field identities, feature/map values, parser order, parameter visibility, remotes, object-info/status authority, and the coordinated rollback unit. |
| Failed output save remains pending forever | Conversion/write/preview/index work throws after `StillSavingFiles` insertion. | Full bytes/tasks remain retained and future path reads can repeatedly serve stale data or fail instead of reading disk. | Background exceptions are logged; successful saves remove after ten seconds. | The only removal is after all fallible work, with no `finally`; both readers prefer the map. | Failure rate and retained volume vary. | Remove exactly once in `finally`, retaining the successful ten-second window; assess observable save failure separately. | Preserve immediate URLs, read-through behavior, metadata/index order, and path keys. |
| Streaming producer fault transport | A WebSocket producer throws after its route enters `RunWebsocketHandlerCallWS`. | Before implementation, progress could stop with a clean close/final status, or Image Batch could emit contradictory success. | **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** After draining queued frames, the helper logs detail, emits one generic `internal_error`, returns `false`, and owners suppress contradictory finalization; T2I separately drains helper-task exceptions before rethrow. | The exact range `5511d3a6^..84df8dfe`, five-file scope, one-definition/six-call inventory, result/error ordering, owner branches, successful paths, and unchanged direct/route/browser contracts are statically confirmed; maintainer Reaper176 confirmed the complete Linux matrix on 2026-07-25. | Windows/other-platform behavior, fault frequency, and performance are unvalidated. | Preserve the passed Linux fault, coordination, compatibility/security, and success matrix when changing this transport. | Preserve queued-frame order, T2I socket reuse, route contracts, normal close behavior, uninterpreted readable errors, escaping sends, and the precompiled-binary caveat. |
| Canceled model-load scheduling strands accounting | Session interruption cancels the scheduling token after pressure claims are created but before the delegate starts. | `LoadingModels` and session claims remain elevated; shared pressure can remain loading while other requests wait. | Cleanup is complete when the delegate starts; sole-request completion can remove its pressure entry but not the claims. | All resets/disposals are inside a cancellation-gated delegate. | The pre-start race window is runtime-dependent. | Make cleanup unavoidable and exactly once without changing scheduling policy. | Preserve pressure heuristic, load cancellation, status frames, reservations, and failure reasons. |
