| Workflow extraction breaks graph/extension contracts | A broad decomposition changes state, priorities, IDs, rewrites, or public members. | Generated graphs or external steps change behavior despite compiling. | **Implemented; partially validated under a one-time maintainer-authorized agent override.** The internal editor owns only graph primitives behind the unchanged facade. | The exact six-commit/two-file projection, unchanged public declarations and maintained consumers, stage reviews/parity, final 0-warning/0-error build, and cases 1–24 exact graph/state/error parity are recorded in Comfy F26. | Cases 25–42 remain unrun; representative model/workflow-family and unknown external-extension behavior are not established. | Run or separately disposition the unchanged representative workflow cases 25–42 before claiming complete validation. | Preserve facade, public fields/lists/constructor/methods, order, reserved IDs, JSON, direct mutation, and rollback stages exactly. |

The Image Editing and image-history lifecycle checks did not support additional confirmed leak/duplicate-handler risks. Editor redraw intervals are cleared on deactivation; image-history scroll listeners are detached on reattachment, resize registration is deduplicated, retry/watchdog timers are cleared, bulk busy state uses `finally`, and its header event binding is guarded by readiness state. CSS media rules, fixed overlays, z-index layers, and important declarations remain compatibility context, not entries in this register.

## Performance Opportunities and Measurement Requirements

All eight retained candidates are classified **Plausible material cost requiring measurement before change**. Their repeated work is structurally confirmed, but its materiality is not. No candidate currently qualifies as **Confirmed structural waste suitable for behavior-preserving removal** because each algorithm change still needs workload evidence, and the explicitly dismissed file-size/count/style claims are **Rejected speculative micro-optimization**. Measurements authorize a later design only when the named cost contributes materially to the stated latency, CPU, allocation, memory, lock, or frame-time boundary; they never authorize a behavior change directly.

### Client P1 — full image-history response processing and DOM replacement

- **Entry point and frequency:** `ImageHistoryController.listFolderAndFiles` runs on initial navigation, folder navigation, filters, sorts, and refreshes; the fast-first root path immediately schedules `queueFullLoad`, which performs a second full response and `replaceBrowserContents`.
- **Cardinality/configured bound:** fast-first is capped at 128. The background `ListImages` request supplies no explicit general client limit, but `ImageHistoryAPI` caps returned files at the user's `MaxImagesInHistory` setting (default 1,000) on both indexed and scan paths. Its `data.files` length is therefore the configured returned cap or the smaller matching response, not total history cardinality.
- **Execution and existing mitigation:** response processing is synchronous on the browser thread: folder sort, two-pass pre/post ordering, mapping, optional client sort, grid filtering, then `GenPageBrowserClass.build` and `lastFilesMap` construction. The network request is asynchronous. Existing mitigations include fast-first paint, server-side filter text, server-supported sorts, a bounded metadata cache, stale-response tokens, browser list cache, and explicit request/map/render timing logs.
- **Certainty/category:** the repeated linear passes, optional `O(n log n)` client sort, and full replacement are **statically confirmed**. Material user impact is a **plausible material cost requiring measurement**, not a confirmed bottleneck.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** record existing `History load`/`History background load` request, map, and render fields plus main-thread long tasks for representative small, medium, and largest maintainer histories, each view mode, structured/text filters, client-only sorts, refresh, and folder depth. Separate server time from map/render. Only if client map/render is material should a future design reduce passes, incrementally reconcile results, or bound/virtualize DOM work; it must preserve ordering, selection, optimistic insertion, compatibility accessors, and stale-response behavior.

### Client P2 — visible-history window recomputes layout for every rendered entry

- **Entry point and frequency:** `ImageHistoryWindowManager.attach` binds history-content scroll and window resize; each event is coalesced to one animation frame by `queueUpdate`, and browser builds/tab shows also queue an update.
- **Cardinality/configured bound:** `updateVisibleWindow` converts every content child with `data-name`, reads every entry's `offsetTop`/`offsetHeight` to rebuild rows, scans from both ends for the visible range, then walks every row to hydrate/dehydrate. Work scales with all rendered history entries, up to the configured `MaxImagesInHistory` response cap (default 1,000) or the smaller matching response.
- **Execution and existing mitigation:** this is synchronous main-thread DOM/layout work. Request-animation-frame coalescing prevents duplicate same-frame updates, the row buffer reduces media churn, and off-window image sources are removed. `BrowserMediaWindowManager` provides a similar cached-owner pattern for model browsers, but neither implementation caches row geometry across scroll events.
- **Certainty/category:** the `O(n)` scans and layout reads per queued update are **statically confirmed**; whether they affect scrolling on supported devices is a **plausible material cost requiring measurement**.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** profile scroll/resize frame time and forced-layout events at increasing rendered entry counts and view widths, with animated/still media and after a full background load. If material, evaluate caching row geometry until build/resize or using a bounded observer/virtualization seam; preserve hydration distance, selected/current card DOM, browser events, and fallback behavior. If measurements remain below the maintainer's responsiveness threshold, reject the change.

### Core P3 — request and conditional late prompt conversion synchronously resolve file-backed media

- **Invocation and frequency:** `RequestToParams` applies submitted values and, when enabled, selected presets through `T2IParamTypes.ApplyParameter`/`ValidateParam` once in each maintained outer context: core `GenT2I_Internal`, `ImageHistoryAPI.AddImageToHistory`, `ComfyUIWebAPI.ComfyGetGeneratedWorkflow`, Grid `GridGenRun`, and Image Batch `GenBatchRun_Internal`. Grid passes `applyPresets: false`, then can apply pending/requested presets per cell before task creation. Separately, the normal `T2IEngine.CreateImageTask` path calls `ApplyLateSpecialLogic` before backend acquisition; conditional `<preset>` and `<param>` prompt tags can re-enter the same conversion on per-image/per-cell/per-file inputs. Comfy workflow preview and `ModelsAPI.TestPromptFill` also invoke that late parser once outside engine scheduling.
- **Cardinality, execution, and mitigation:** path-backed `IMAGE`/`AUDIO`/`VIDEO` values convert once per application and `IMAGE_LIST` once per item; late multiplicity depends on matching tags and their file-backed values, so not every task performs this work. Outer converted values survive input cloning. A late/grid reapplication is not content-memoized across inputs: `FilePathToDataString` root-checks the user path, synchronously waits on the shared `StillSavingFiles` task or reads the file, then base64-encodes it. Core/Grid/Image Batch hold a `GenClaim` during outer conversion, and late conversion occurs inside the engine task before backend wait; history/workflow conversion has no generation backend.
- **Certainty and decision gate:** these invocation mechanisms are **statically confirmed**; media/tag frequency and material cost are runtime-dependent. Measure four groups separately: frequency/cardinality (outer calls, grid cells, tasks, matching tags/items); bytes/source (unique/repeated paths, pending-save versus filesystem); time/allocation (wait, read, encode, allocations); and placement (claim-held outer time and engine-start-to-backend-request time). Include workflow preview and `TestPromptFill`. Only if material repeated cost is demonstrated should a request/claim-scoped authorized content cache or async pre-resolution be designed.
- **Compatibility/non-goals:** preserve path authorization/freshness, pending-save and data-URL semantics, prompt-tag order, per-task clone isolation, Grid presets, cancellation/claim accounting, metadata, and backend contracts; reject a broad async rewrite or blanket all-task cost claim.

### Core P4 — model sidecars are read and parsed twice during metadata-cache recomputation

- **Status, entry point, and frequency:** **Measurement-gated and unimplemented.** `T2IModelHandler.Refresh` recursively scans model folders and calls `AddAllFromFolder`, which calls `LoadMetadata` for discovered models. A missing metadata record, changed model-file mtime, legacy cache condition, legacy null sidecar fingerprint, or supported-sidecar fingerprint mismatch enters the recomputation branch. For each existing suffix in `AltModelMetadataJsonFileSuffixes`, one loop reads/parses/merges JSON into metadata and a later loop reads/parses the same sidecar again for `procAltHeader`.
- **Cardinality/configured bound:** duplicated work is once per recomputed model times each of four configured alternate metadata suffixes that exists. Folder recursion and model discovery run in parallel. Rank 22's ordered sidecar fingerprint adds bounded filesystem metadata inspection to the reuse decision and causes ordinary sidecar add/edit/delete changes to recompute; it does not cache or reuse parsed objects. A reusable record still bypasses recomputation, so duplicate content reads/parses are not paid on every listing.
- **Execution and existing mitigation:** both passes synchronously read text and parse `JObject`; the first merges sidecar properties into the embedded header data, while the second extracts display name, description, and trigger phrases that are later persisted with the metadata cache record. The LiteDB record gated by model-file last-write time plus the optional ordered sidecar fingerprint and parallel folder scanning mitigates steady-state refresh cost but does not reuse the already parsed object during recomputation.
- **Certainty/category:** duplicate sidecar I/O and parsing in the same recomputation branch are **statically confirmed**. Aggregate user impact depends on recomputed model count, sidecar prevalence/size, and filesystem latency; materiality is unknown.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** time missing-record, reusable-record, changed-model-mtime, changed-sidecar-fingerprint, legacy-null-fingerprint, and legacy-`TextEncoders` refresh cases while recording model count, recomputed count, existing suffix count/bytes, storage type, sidecar read/parse time, fingerprint-inspection time, and total refresh/event latency. Only if recomputation cost is material should a bounded change parse each existing sidecar once and reuse its `JObject`, with parity checks for merge order, invalid JSON handling, `ModelFileVersion`, sidecar fingerprint behavior, the legacy condition, `procAltHeader`, and emitted metadata. Defer a maintainability-only duplicate-pass cleanup and reject broader scan/cache redesign without supporting measurements. Rank 22 corrects Core F12's cache-freshness contract but neither implements nor authorizes this optimization.

### Backend P5 — the request loop repeatedly rebuilds and filters scheduler snapshots

- **Invocation path and frequency source:** `BackendHandler.RequestHandlingLoop` wakes on backend/request/release signals or after one second and iterates a `ToArray` snapshot of every pending request. Each `T2IBackendRequest.TryFind` rebuilds a list of all T2I backends, filters possible/available instances, sorts available instances by usage, and may filter by request matcher. A model miss enters `LoadHighestPressureNow`, which snapshots and sorts all non-loading model pressures and performs nested pressure-request/available-loader compatibility checks. Pending requests without progress repeat on later passes.
- **Cardinality and synchronous/shared-state behavior:** work scales with pending requests, configured/runtime backends, requested feature/model filter cost, and active distinct model pressures. The loop is one synchronous thread over concurrent catalogs; per-pressure mutation uses a lock only on selected paths. Existing `CheckBackendsSignal`, one-second fallback, already-loaded-model fast path, pressure grouping, autoscaling gates, and opt-in `BackendQueueTimer` marks mitigate or expose the cost.
- **Static certainty/category:** repeated snapshot/filter/sort mechanisms and multiplicity are **statically confirmed**. Queue sizes, wake rate, matcher cost, contention, and user-visible scheduler delay are unknown, so this is a **plausible material cost requiring measurement before change**. The concurrency defects in F17/F18 do not prove this path is slow.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** use the existing timer boundary plus counters for loop passes/wakes, pending request count, backend count, pressure count/request membership, `TryFind` time, matcher time, pressure-selection time, allocation, and time from signal to claim. Cover idle, one model already loaded, many same-model requests, many distinct models, mixed feature refusals, autoscaling, cancellation, and backend release. Only if scheduler CPU/allocation or claim latency is material should a later design consider sharing a per-pass snapshot of backend identity/catalog membership, processing only signaled state transitions, or centralizing pressure selection. Catalog sharing must not freeze availability: requests are processed sequentially, and a successful `TryFind` immediately changes usage through `T2IBackendAccess`, while reservations, status, loaded model, model pressure, and matcher-visible state can also change during the pass. Every request must therefore re-evaluate usage/capacity, reservations, status, matcher/feature/model acceptance, and current-model preference against the latest state unless a future scheduler design proves identical state transitions and selection for every interleaving. Preserve request iteration and extension `CanTryFindNow` order, usage-based choice, model-pressure heuristic/order, refusal reasons, autoscaler triggers, cancellation/timeouts, and public facades; measurements do not authorize stale capacity/availability reuse or reordered claims.

### Backend P6 — each persisted output rescans its folder and lifetime reservation keys

- **Invocation path and frequency source:** this path runs only for outputs that proceed through persisted saving. In normal generation, `T2IAPI.saveImage` bypasses `Session.SaveImage` for `DoNotSave` and for non-real outputs covered by `DoNotSaveIntermediates`; if it calls `Session.SaveImage`, user `SaveFiles = false` returns a data URL before output-path resolution, `UserLock`, or either scan. With saving enabled, `Session.SaveImage` synchronously resolves the output template/path/format, then under `User.UserLock` calls `Directory.EnumerateFiles(folderRoute)`, filters all `RecentlyBlockedFilenames.Keys` by folder prefix, unions both sets, strips extensions into a new hash set, and probes/reserves the requested name before returning its persisted URL. Normal `T2IEngine` reaches that branch from `handleFileOutput` inside the `GenerateLive` callback and backend-access `using`, once per accepted output that is actually persisted; the optional mini-grid is handled later outside that engine backend scope. Image History add shares `Session.SaveImage` and bypasses its scans when user saving is disabled. Grid Generator uses a data URL when its input `DoNotSave` is true and otherwise calls `Session.SaveImage`, whose user-level saving bypass still applies.
- **Cardinality and synchronous/shared-state behavior:** for persisted outputs, filesystem enumeration scales with current files in the target folder, and the dictionary scan scales with process-lifetime distinct saved/deleted paths (F20). For normal generated outputs that persist, path selection, both scans, reservation, and `UserLock` hold all occur while backend usage remains claimed; direct data-URL bypasses perform none of that work. Different users may run concurrently. After reservation, the background task's conversion await, media/sidecar write, preview, index upsert, and ten-second pending-byte retention outlive the callback and can outlive backend release. The output-template `[number]` token, existing-file check, reservation set, and lock are the current collision controls; the history LiteDB index is not an authoritative live directory snapshot.
- **Static certainty/category:** repeated full folder/key scans and their per-output multiplicity are **statically confirmed**. Filesystem/cache sizes, storage latency, lock wait, naming-collision frequency, and request impact require runtime evidence, so algorithm replacement is a **plausible material cost requiring measurement**. F20 independently justifies bounding reservation lifetime; it does not justify caching the directory.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** record persisted-versus-data-URL disposition (including each bypass reason), target-folder file count, reservation-key count/matches, path-resolution plus enumeration/key-scan/hash/probe time, `UserLock` hold/wait time, backend-claim extension attributable to synchronous persisted-save selection, batch size, storage type, naming pattern, and end-to-end save-callback latency for empty, typical, large flat, deeply partitioned, repeated-name, concurrent-save, and delete/regenerate cases. Measure background conversion/write/preview/index separately rather than attributing it to backend claim time, and exclude bypassed outputs from scan timing. After F20 bounds stale keys, optimize further only if the remaining synchronous persisted-output scan materially delays output publication or backend release; any per-folder allocator/snapshot must reconcile external files and preserve cross-extension collisions, `[number]`/suffix semantics, multiple users, unsafe-path policy, deletion races, all data-URL bypasses, and rollback to direct enumeration.
- **Rank 25 collection outcome:** maintainer Reaper176's one-time override authorized the agent to build and run the exact reviewed temporary instrumentation outside repository user-data paths. The Garuda Linux (Arch-based), Btrfs matrix recorded 23 passed, 0 failed, and 2 unrun: the workflow-dependent intermediate-output case and true two-user concurrency remain unrun and uninferred. External privacy review accepted 73 schema-1 records with SHA-256 `7a9d23d7238a226e334e40baabb975716e414b975ea504a879dd1cfb9d839ccd`. Using nearest-rank order statistics, thirty-four persisted selections had a 124 us p50, 2,157 us p95, and 2,667 us maximum; nine backend-claimed selections had a 158 us p50 and 381 us maximum. The 5,000-file flat folder selected in 2,157 us, dominated by its 2,019 us directory scan/hash, while preview and history-index p95 values were 55,256 us and 12,826 us. The decision is **NO-GO**: the scan scales, but filename selection was not repeatedly material in the representative collection, so no allocator/cache optimization is authorized. Removal commit `ee4888f263d649d5888676610bc562ff6104d969` restores the complete `src` tree to design commit `30448884415c44f446136fa3e11fb06cefe375d6`; the static removal gate and maintainer-authorized agent-run persisted-save/data-URL sanity returned `RANK25_INSTRUMENTATION_REMOVAL_STATIC_GATE_PASSED` and `RANK25_INSTRUMENTATION_REMOVAL_PASSED`.

### Comfy P7 — final graph cleanup repeatedly scans and rebuilds the workflow

- **Invocation path and frequency source:** every standard generated workflow executes the priority-200 post-cleanup step in `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/WorkflowGeneratorSteps.cs::Register`; stored/raw custom workflows bypass the generator and this cost. The step calls `WorkflowGenerator.RunOnNodesOfClass` for `SwarmKSampler`, `LTXVSeparateAVLatent`, two decode classes, and two encode classes. Each call materializes a full `Workflow.Properties()` scan; every matched `ReplaceNodeConnection` scans every node and its direct input properties. It then calls `RemoveClassesIfUnused`, which repeats until stable and rebuilds a workflow-wide direct-connection set once per pass (`WorkflowGenerator.cs`).
- **Cardinality, synchronous/shared-state, and cache behavior:** work occurs synchronously after `T2IEngine` has acquired/claimed the selected backend but before the JSON graph is submitted to Comfy. Six base class scans scale with graph node count; connection replacements scale with matching rewrites times all nodes/direct inputs; cleanup scales with eligible nodes and the number of fixed-point passes. `UsedInputs` avoids rebuilding within one cleanup pass and is invalidated between passes. The graph is request-local, but global step registration determines whether and when cleanup runs.
- **Static certainty/category:** the repeated scan/rewrite multiplicity is **statically confirmed**. Typical node counts, matched rewrite counts, fixed-point depth, allocation, and contribution to request latency are unknown, so this is a **plausible material cost requiring measurement**. F26's ownership benefit does not prove the path is slow.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** record per generated workflow total node/direct-input count before cleanup, count/time for each of the six class scans, matching nodes, connection replacements and examined properties, eligible cleanup nodes, fixed-point passes, removed nodes, allocations, total generator/cleanup time, selected-backend claim time consumed before prompt submission, and serialized workflow size. Cover minimal still, SDXL/refiner, LoRA/control/adapter, regional/segmentation, video/audio, intermediate-output, and Dynamic Thresholding graphs. Only if cleanup is material should F26's graph editor maintain a request-local class/connection index or combine traversals; it must preserve snapshot iteration, rewrite order, direct-array connection semantics, cleanup fixed point, node/property order, IDs, extension mutations, and rollback to scans.

### Comfy P8 — the first workflow-list request after reload reads and parses every stored graph

- **Invocation path and frequency source:** `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIBackendExtension.cs::OnPreInit` and each `Refresh` call `LoadWorkflowFiles`, which clears `CustomWorkflows` and inserts a null value for every discovered user/example JSON file. The next `src/BuiltinExtensions/ComfyUIBackend/ComfyUIWebAPI.cs::ComfyListWorkflows` snapshots all keys and calls `GetWorkflowByName` for each; every null entry synchronously reads and parses the complete file, extracts full workflow/prompt/custom parameters/values plus list metadata, and caches the complete record. Later lists reuse records until the next reload; direct read/generation can hydrate individual entries earlier.
- **Cardinality, synchronous/shared-state, and cache behavior:** cold-list work scales with stored workflow count and total JSON bytes, including graph and prompt bodies that the list response does not emit. File reads and Newtonsoft parsing are synchronous in the API handler path. `ConcurrentDictionary` protects entry publication; ordering and response construction allocate lists/JObjects; failed entries log and are omitted but remain null for later retries. The full-record cache makes warm listing cheap and supports generation without a second read.
- **Static certainty/category:** cold all-entry read/parse and its multiplicity are **statically confirmed**. Workflow counts/sizes, refresh frequency, filesystem latency, and user-visible delay are unknown, so materiality requires measurement. F23 independently justifies a workflow-store owner but does not authorize changing hydration.
- **Required measurement and decision gate:** measure cold and warm list request time, per-file read/parse time, total bytes, workflow count, allocation/retained record size, refresh-to-first-list delay, concurrent list/read/generation, invalid files, examples, and local/network storage. Only if cold latency or retained memory is material should the F23 store expose a metadata descriptor/cache that avoids hydrating graph bodies for listing. Preserve the single JSON format, sorted list fields, lazy per-workflow read, invalid-file logging/omission, immediate successful-save visibility, refresh behavior, public dictionary compatibility, and rollback to full-record hydration.

### Rejected frontend micro-optimization claims

No finding is retained from JavaScript file size, top-level symbol count, inline-handler count, CSS `!important`/z-index/fixed-position counts, or the existence of JSON/DOM/local-storage operations alone. In particular, replacing the history controller's small literal maps, changing `orderFilesForDisplay` solely to avoid one array pass, or converting classic scripts/modules without measured benefit would be speculative micro-optimization or compatibility-expanding redesign. Those ideas remain rejected unless runtime evidence supplies a material cost and a bounded owner.

### Rejected core architecture and performance claims

No finding is retained from raw `Utilities.cs`/`BackendHandler.cs` size, public-registry/static-field counts, synchronous waits/threads, autoscaler tick existence, or repeated serialization/list construction without a traced consumer and failure or cost mechanism. In particular, this audit rejects a bulk Utilities split, one combined backend lifecycle-and-scheduling rewrite, broad API/parameter/model registry encapsulation, removal of `Program` statics based only on count, an async rewrite based only on `.Wait`/`.Result` occurrence, optimization of `ListT2IParams` or backend status caching without measurements, and removal of the T2I compatibility facade. Scheduling-policy extraction and backend lifecycle/persistence extraction remain separate possible seams; neither is authorized until the reliability fixes and Backend P5 measurements establish a bounded first owner. Those broader proposals would expand compatibility and migration scope beyond the evidence above.

### Rejected Comfy/Python architecture and performance claims

No finding is retained merely from the size of `ComfyUIBackendExtension`, `WorkflowGenerator`, its partial files, public member counts, anonymous step count, Python `print`/exception style, or the number of node schemas. The completed catalogs are not reopened for Python code generation, runtime reflection, a universal upstream-node catalog, or cosmetic constant coverage. This audit also rejects moving all Comfy parameters/storage/install/update/transport out of the extension at once, replacing the `JObject` graph with a typed graph, reordering or naming all workflow steps, changing node IDs/property order, making optional imports mandatory, or optimizing object-info/model-list parsing without measurements. The only first workflow ownership seam retained is F26's facade-preserving graph editor, and P7/P8 authorize no algorithm or cache change until their stated measurements are material.

## Ranked Refactoring Roadmap

Ranks apply the approved order—compatibility/reliability, ownership/maintainability, measured performance, then cosmetics—before leverage and feasibility. “Exact evidence” below points to the fully recorded F/P entry, whose pinned paths, symbols, complete maintained consumer boundary, current flow, and certainty remain part of the project specification. A project may not absorb a neighboring rank merely because the same subsystem is open. The original roadmap remains 24 production projects plus eight measurement prerequisites, or 32 numbered ranks. Unranked security prerequisite `S1` was discovered during rank-2 verification, directly continues that confidentiality boundary, and is implemented and maintainer-validated without renumbering or increasing the ranked counts; rank 3 is now unblocked.

### 1. Redact shared browser API diagnostics

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented, awaiting maintainer validation.** The retained entry documents its compatibility, validation, success, and rollback boundary; it is no longer the recommended next production project.
- **Boundary and owner:** `src/wwwroot/js/site.js::genericRequest`; commit `0d7076ce` removed full `in_data` serialization. **Exact evidence and consumers:** Frontend F4; `SetAPIKey` supplies `key`, every request gains `session_id`, and the maintained HTTP consumers span core pages, generation/history/model utilities, and built-ins.
- **Payoff:** high credential/privacy reliability, medium transport maintainability, no performance claim; high leverage, high feasibility, low regression risk. **Prerequisites:** retain endpoint-generated `data.error` verbatim, UI/callback behavior, retry semantics, and arbitrary extension payload compatibility.
- **Implemented stage/non-goals:** only the client's request-value serialization was removed; transport, UI errors, and server endpoint error text remain unchanged. **Verification/validation:** trace client payload logs; reject API-key and image/metadata requests, then trigger invalid Image History sort. **Success:** client diagnostics add no request values beyond unchanged server-provided `data.error`; UI/server error behavior is identical. **Rollback:** restore only a value-free client diagnostic statement, never arbitrary request serialization.

### 2. Introduce redacted Comfy workflow diagnostics

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented, awaiting maintainer validation.** No live validation is claimed.
- **Boundary and owner:** `ComfyDiagnostics` owns safe workflow, typed-parameter, and exception representations; source owners use fixed text for tag and private-data failures. `ComfySubmittedJson` owns the fresh fixed-message `JsonReaderException` boundary and never retains the original as an inner exception. The expanded production owners are `ComfyUIAPIAbstractBackend`, `ComfyUIBackendExtension`, `ComfyUIWebAPI`, `ComfyUIRedirectHelper`, `ComfyUser`, and `WorkflowGeneratorSteps` (six files). **Evidence/consumers:** Comfy F25 preserves the exact nine-original-statement inventory and separately records 12 maintained submitted/private parsing or unescaping invocations. The raw browser-to-Swarm Comfy WebSocket statement removed from `ComfyUser` is additional to the original nine.
- **Payoff:** high server-log/error-surface confidentiality, high diagnostic consistency, no performance claim; high leverage, medium feasibility, medium risk because failure diagnostics and valid control flow must remain useful. **Protected paths:** `AwaitJobLive`, `TryIsValid`, tag unescape, submitted dynamic metadata, persisted private custom-workflow container, four save fields, direct prompt, direct interrupt, and browser-to-Swarm Comfy WebSocket.
- **Implemented stages/non-goals:** the ten protected statement lines now use seven formatter calls or fixed text. Workflow summaries validate the complete document with `DateParseHandling.None` and retain only ordinal aliases, counts, fixed statuses/kinds, and aliased source edges; semantic identifiers and output indexes are omitted. Parameters expose only their count; tag, direct-prompt, browser WebSocket, raw/stored tag-processing, metadata conversion, stored conversion, and `TryIsValid` failure paths use fixed or ordinal-only diagnostics. All 12 source boundaries use `ComfySubmittedJson`, raw `ComfyUser` content is no longer logged, and the dormant unsafe metadata-log example is removed. Submission JSON, successful tag substitution, log levels, readable ControlNet error, and valid operation/control flow remain out of scope for change. Comfy backend-response parsers, `/prompt` responses, output/history diagnostics, and returned metadata remain excluded by provenance and unchanged.
- **Verification/validation:** static checks inventory nine original statements, ten protected lines, seven formatter calls, and 12 invocations; verify EOF/date-neutral diagnostic parsing, opaque aliases and aliased edges, count-only parameters, fixed private failures, shape-safe `TryIsValid`, fresh inner-free parser exceptions, six production-owner migrations, unsafe-log removal, and unchanged backend-response paths. Maintainer sentinel validation must cover raw workflow parsing, malformed raw/stored workflow validation in `TryIsValid`, all four save fields, valid-JSON metadata conversion, stored private container and `enable_in_simple` conversion, tag escape, direct prompt, direct interrupt, browser WebSocket, preview, generation/prompt/transport failures, direct proxy, ControlNet with no usable ControlNet/init image, ISO-looking connected node IDs, and supported log levels. **Success:** generated graph order/counts and aliased connection edges, broad non-private error detail, and the ControlNet missing-image error remain, while no submitted sentinel, semantic identifier, output index, source preview, parser/conversion message/path/line detail, raw WebSocket text, or typed-input/base64 value appears. **Rollback:** restore only fixed/opaque structural or broad non-private verbosity; never restore semantic identifiers, output indexes, raw values/messages, parser/conversion details, or content-bearing inner exceptions.

### 3. Align OAuth login cookie transport flags

- **Implementation status:** **Implemented and maintainer-validated.** The OAuth writer sets `Secure = HttpContext.Request.IsHttps`, exactly matching the request-aware decision used by password login and logout. Cookie name/value, HttpOnly, one-year expiry, SameSite, session creation/storage/validation, redirect, OAuth registration, readers, and protected consumers are unchanged. - **Post-warm results and gate:** nearest-rank `x[ceil(pN)]`, without interpolation, gives active pass (`N=52`) p50/p95/max 64/1123/1413 microseconds and 4,880/142,368/142,368 bytes; `try_find` (`N=300`) 7/26/1381 microseconds and 2,056/4,144/5,720 bytes, including snapshot/filter 2/5/8 and availability/sort 1/7/17 microseconds; pressure (`N=27`) 27/121/146 microseconds and 4,744/28,792/28,792 bytes; signal-to-first-claim (`N=44`) 110/375/1401 microseconds. Nested scopes overlap. Distinct-model 32-request/32-backend passes are p50/max 388/422 microseconds, same-model 32-pending passes 233/243, and the 32-pressure/four-member/32-loader case 121/146 with 1,024 compatibility plus 4,096 perfect calls. Only the deliberately expensive caller matcher repeatedly reached millisecond scale: pass 1307/1413 microseconds, of which the callback took 1222/1350. A disabled-then-enabled 32-backend direct control measured 10.293 microseconds and 4,924.656 bytes disabled versus 32.670 microseconds and 10,036.384 bytes enabled, so allocation evidence is substantially instrumentation-influenced and lacks production pass-rate/GC context. Repeated built-in work stayed below 0.5 milliseconds in the exercised 32-by-32 queue and pressure stayed below 0.15 milliseconds; the scoped decision is `NO-GO`.
- **Limitations/rollback:** this is synthetic in-memory fake-backend evidence. Direct pressure and an emission-including micro-control were used; no server, generation, GPU, filesystem, real backend lifecycle, production matcher distribution, wake frequency, GC, or other platform/filesystem was exercised. It is not a production-performance or improvement claim. Rollback removes every temporary setting, recorder, signal hook, counter, and timer; that removal and its external post-removal validation remain pending here.

### 27. Measure generated-workflow cleanup traversal cost

- **Disposition/boundary:** **Selected as the sole formal Recommended Next Project; neither designed nor implemented.** Retain as measurement prerequisite for Comfy P7 at priority-200 cleanup, `RunOnNodesOfClass`, `ReplaceNodeConnection`, and `RemoveClassesIfUnused`; standard generated workflows consume it, stored/raw workflows do not. **Evidence:** repeated graph scans/fixed-point rebuilds are confirmed, but materiality remains unknown.
- **Payoff/gate:** possible pre-submit/claimed-backend latency and allocation reduction; high leverage, high graph-regression risk. Change only if cleanup is material and preferably through rank 24's graph editor.
- **Measure/stages:** nodes/direct inputs, six scan times, matches/replacements/examined properties, fixed-point passes/removals, allocation, generator/cleanup/claimed time, serialized size across still/refiner/control/region/video/audio/intermediate/Dynamic Thresholding. Preserve snapshot/rewrite/fixed-point/order/ID semantics. Static verification checks scopes; maintainer compares graphs. Success is decision-quality evidence; rollback removes instrumentation.

- **Disposition/boundary:** **Retain as measurement prerequisite** for Core P3 at `RequestToParams`, Grid preset application, `ApplyLateSpecialLogic`, and `FilePathToDataString`; five outer contexts plus workflow preview and `TestPromptFill` are consumers. **Evidence:** synchronous wait/read/base64 work and multiplicity are confirmed but conditional.

Static verification maps all contexts; maintainer runs media-heavy/grid/batch/preview cases. Success is a bounded cache/pre-resolution decision; rollback removes instrumentation.

- **Retain as measurement prerequisite:** P1-P8, ranks 25-32.
- **Defer as architectural direction:** repository-wide frontend facades/native modules, a `Program` application-services context, broad scheduler-policy/lifecycle separation, workflow collaborators beyond graph primitives, parameter catalog/validation separation, and opportunistic domain utility extraction.
- **Superseded by completed work:** the former roadmap items 1-4 and any proposal to repeat their physical extraction/catalog establishment.
- **Reject as unsupported or cosmetic:** wholesale rewrites; file/line/global/static/public-member counts as defects; standalone `Utilities` or CSS rearrangement; native-module conversion without an extension bridge; broad async conversion; general registry encapsulation; typed Comfy graph replacement; step/ID/order changes; Python style cleanup; object-info/model-list/history micro-optimizations without measurement; and removal of compatibility facades.

The OAuth writer now sets `Secure = HttpContext.Request.IsHttps`, exactly matching the request-aware decision used by password login and logout, without changing cookie/session/authentication consumers or forwarded-header, trusted-proxy, Kestrel, settings, launcher, or Web-pipeline behavior. Direct HTTPS, or trusted hosting that already establishes the ASP.NET request scheme as HTTPS, produces a Secure OAuth cookie; a TLS-terminating proxy whose Swarm-facing request remains HTTP has a separate scheme-establishment limitation, and this change does not trust raw forwarding headers.
